#5052. BlackRock ETF Dumps 1 Million ETH Worth $1.75B as Ethereum Outflows Accelerate: Is ETH In Trouble?

BlackRock’s Ethereum ETF has lost over 1 million ETH worth $1.75 billion since October as outflows accelerate.

The BlackRock ETF has shed more than 1 million ETH since October 1, recording net outflows of 1,018,839 ETH worth approximately $1.75 billion. The selling continued on Monday, as BlackRock’s iShares Ethereum Trust (ETHA) transferred another batch of coins to Coinbase Prime.

Between 11:52 and 12:02 UTC, four separate transfers moved 42,631 ETH from BlackRock’s deposit addresses to Coinbase Prime, the brokerage desk handling the fund’s trades. The transfers were worth approximately $73 million at Monday’s prices.

Since October 1, #BlackRock has seen net outflows of 1,018,839 $ETH ($1.75B).https://t.co/YfaFJXVOID pic.twitter.com/tfljcmoSsM

— Lookonchain (@lookonchain) November 23, 2026

The outflows have taken a large bite out of the fund. Comparing Lookonchain’s data with BlackRock’s own filings confirms the match to the coin. ETHA now holds less Ethereum than it did at the end of June.

How much has the BlackRock ETF lost so far, and what might this mean for ETH?

ETHA held 3,675,697 ETH on September 30, its quarterly report shows. On November 20, the iShares fund page listed 2,656,857 ETH.

That is a drop of 1,018,840 ETH, or 28%, which lines up with Lookonchain’s count almost exactly. Shares outstanding fell by a similar amount, from 162.4 million to 119.1 million, as investors cashed out.

The timing stings. In the third quarter, the BlackRock ETF bought 1.19 million ETH for $2.71 billion as money poured back in. Nearly all of that has now left. ETHA’s 2,656,857 ETH is below the 2,695,456 it held on June 30.

The fund’s net assets stood at $4.31 billion on November 20. Its net asset value per share has fallen 41% this year.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best Low-Cap Crypto

We traced the Arkham-labelled addresses in Lookonchain’s screenshot on the Ethereum blockchain. Two BlackRock deposit addresses, 0xC8c5…5b95 and 0x01c2…59E5, sent every transfer to two Coinbase Prime hot wallets.

Since October 1, the two addresses made 84 transfers totalling 1,128,667 ETH. Priced at the hour each one moved, that is about $2.54 billion, or an average of $2,254 per ETH.

The pace picked up in November. The weeks starting November 2 and November 9 each saw more than 200,000 ETH leave, compared with 120,000 to 170,000 a week in October. The biggest single days were October 7, November 10 and November 20, at more than 70,000 ETH each.

The on-chain total is higher than the net drop in holdings because some new money still came in. It also includes Monday’s transfers, which are not yet in the fund’s November 20 figures.

These moves do not mean BlackRock is selling for itself. Coinbase acts as the trust’s agent, and ETHA pays out redemptions either in ETH or in cash raised by selling ETH. When investors leave the fund, the coins go out through Coinbase Prime.

EXPLORE: BitMine’s Estimated $3.6B ETH Loss Overshadows Stock Edge

ETH trades at $1,724, below its 50-day exponential moving average (EMA), an average that gives recent prices more weight, at $2,131, and its 200-day EMA at $2,248. It fell to $1,538 on November 21, just above its June low of $1,506.

Monday’s 42,631 ETH shows the BlackRock ETF redemptions have not stopped. A pause in transfers to Coinbase Prime would be the first sign the selling is easing.

#5051. Pump.fun’s price surges – But THESE signals keep PUMP bears in control

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Pump.fun [PUMP] is up 4.74% over the past 24 hours as of writing, a slightly more positive gain compared to the rest of the crypto market, which rose 2.34%. While there is no verified catalyst behind the rally, PUMP’s reported cross-margin and Pro mode rollout may have sparked interest in the token again. Furthermore, the broader market’s recovery and a bid-heavy order book could be another reason for the surge.

However, the recent rally is looking cautiously optimistic, as the recovery is yet to overcome the bearish trend, leaving PUMP at considerable risk of another pullback in the near term.

On the daily chart, PUMP was trading at $0.002568 at the time of writing and approaching the 23.6% Fibonacci resistance at $0.002745. However, the broader price structure remains firmly bearish, with the token continuing to make lower highs and lower lows.

Reclaiming this Fibonacci level would be an initial sign that buyers are gaining ground, but the chances of that happening in the near term appear to be slim to none.

The indicators were also pointing to a potential near-term reversal with an RSI of 31.53 and OBV continuing its downtrend, indicating that sellers are still in control of the market. This makes it difficult for the recent price recovery to develop into a sustained reversal.

The four-hour chart offered a slightly more encouraging picture. After recovering over the past two days, PUMP reclaimed the 61.8% Fibonacci level at $0.002554 and entered the golden pocket. However, it has since started to retest the support, making this an important area to defend. Holding above it could open the door to $0.002654, the next Fibonacci resistance.

The MACD showed improving momentum, with its line above the signal line, although both remained below zero. H4 OBV also maintained its broader upward trend despite a recent dip, suggesting buying interest had eased slightly.

It’s absolutely vital that the bulls defend $0.002554 successfully, as it would extend the recovery toward $0.002745. Losing this support, on the other hand, would expose $0.002484 and potentially further losses.

#5050. 'I Hate Risk’: Ripple CTO Emeritus Explains XRP and Bitcoin Sell-Off

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

David Schwartz, CTO Emeritus at Ripple and one of the creators of the XRP Ledger, has once again commented on his XRP and Bitcoin sales. This time, the reason was a viral post on X that claimed he had sold almost all of his crypto over the last few months.

According to the post, Schwartz's portfolio reportedly went from 26 million XRP, 1,500 BTC and 40,000 ETH to just 3 million XRP, 1 BTC and 6 ETH. The author called it "an insane reduction" and also referred to Schwartz as the current CTO of Ripple, while he now holds the emeritus title.

Schwartz replied that the timeline in the post was wrong. "That wasn't in the last few months, that was in the last decade," he wrote. As proof, he shared his own post from April 21, 2019. In that post, Schwartz said he was still sticking to the "de-risking strategy" that he and his wife agreed on when they first decided to put their own money into crypto. He also admitted back then that selling during the bull run "still hurt."

At the same time, Schwartz did not dispute the numbers themselves. "But it's basically true. I hate risk. I can't help it," he wrote in a follow-up post. According to him, pretty much every big risk he has ever taken has worked out well, and rationally he should be taking more risk as he gets wealthier. "But I hate it. I just hate risk and can't do anything about it," Schwartz added.

That wasn't in the last few months, that was in the last decade. You can find me talking about this in many posts over the years, like this one:https://t.co/5MgcPZ5qyG

At press time, XRP was trading at around $0.939, Bitcoin at $61,477 and Ethereum at $1,722. At these prices, the remaining 3 million XRP, 1 BTC and 6 ETH are worth about $2.89 million, and XRP accounts for more than 97% of this sum.

For comparison, at current prices, the peak holdings mentioned in the post would be worth over $185 million, with Bitcoin alone accounting for about half of it.

Schwartz has already explained his sales several times this year. In May, he said he did not have much XRP left and had tried to move most of his assets, other than Ripple stock, away from crypto exposure. In July, he said he regrets selling XRP at $0.10 and ETH at around $1, but noted that he and his wife had agreed to sell at every new all-time high.

Earlier in November, Schwartz disclosed that he sold about 2.5 million XRP in 2025. Per Schwartz, most of these tokens were received in 2020 and 2023, and he paid approximately $1.7 million in capital gains tax on those sales. He shared this in response to a user who called XRP a "terrible investment."

Among other things, Schwartz has previously said that his main exposure to the crypto sector now comes through Ripple stock, not XRP. In March, Ripple launched a share buyback of up to $750 million that valued the company at $50 billion.

#5049. Pulsar Enables USDT Deposits on TRON, Expanding Access to Everyday Stablecoin Payments

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Geneva, Switzerland, November 19, 2026 — Pulsar Money today announced support for USDT deposits on the TRON network, allowing users to fund their Pulsar accounts with USDT (TRC-20) and access everyday payment features through the app.

With this integration, users can deposit USDT on TRON and use their balances for online purchases, subscriptions, and other everyday expenses through the Pulsar virtual card. Pulsar charges no fees for supported stablecoin deposits across its plans, although network and third-party fees may apply.

The integration connects TRON’s widely adopted stablecoin infrastructure with Pulsar’s consumer-focused payment platform. In Q3 2026, TRON processed $2.21 trillion in stablecoin settlement volume, averaging $24 billion daily. By supporting USDT deposits on TRON, Pulsar offers users a convenient way to put their existing stablecoin holdings to use for everyday payments.

“Supporting USDT on TRON gives people another way to bring the stablecoins they already use into Pulsar,” said Alex, Founder and CEO of Pulsar Money. “Our focus is on making those balances useful in everyday life, whether people are managing their money, shopping online, or paying for subscriptions.”

“TRON’s extensive stablecoin activity reflects the growing demand for accessible, efficient digital payments,” said Sam Elfarra, Community Spokesperson for TRON DAO. “Pulsar’s integration brings that infrastructure closer to everyday use, expanding how people can access and spend their stablecoins.”

Pulsar combines stablecoin balance management and payment features in a single app. To deposit USDT on TRON, users can select the supported asset and network in the Pulsar app and follow the deposit instructions. Users should ensure they select TRON (TRC-20) and send only USDT to the deposit address provided for that network. Features and availability may vary by region and service provider.

Pulsar Money is a money app that brings stablecoins and everyday payment features together. Users can manage supported balances and access services including a virtual card and currency conversion, subject to availability and provider terms. Pulsar is not a bank.

TRON DAO is a community-governed DAO dedicated to accelerating the decentralization of the internet via blockchain technology and dApps.

Founded in September 2017, the TRON blockchain has experienced significant growth since its MainNet launch in May 2018. Today, TRON hosts the largest circulating supply of USD Tether (USDT) stablecoin, which currently exceeds $94 billion. As of November 2026, the TRON blockchain has recorded over 415 million in total user accounts, more than 16 billion in total transactions, and over $24 billion in total value locked (TVL), based on TRONSCAN. Recognized as the global settlement layer for stablecoin transactions and everyday purchases with proven success, TRON is “Moving Trillions, Empowering Billions.”

#5048. Butter Capital Launches Multichain USDC and USDT Staking Powered by 8 Model Butter Cortex AI

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The Butter Capital Limited, an active company limited by shares incorporated in the British Virgin Islands under Legal Entity Identifier (LEI) 98450051EB909E909F56, has announced the public launch of Butter Capital, a managed 30-day USD stablecoin staking platform supporting USD Coin (USDC) and Tether (USDT) across six EVM networks: Base, Arbitrum, BNB Chain, Polygon, Optimism and Ethereum.

Built for digital asset holders seeking structured stablecoin deployment without manually managing funding intervals, cross-chain bridges, or margin liquidation ladders, Butter Capital allows participants to open 30-day stakes starting from $10 USD. Staked capital backs active execution through Butter Cortex, the platform’s quantitative cryptocurrency perpetual futures consensus desk orchestrating Mythos, Claude, Grok, OpenAI, Gemini, DeepSeek, Perplexity, and Llama.

Users onboard in seconds via cryptographic Web3 wallet sign-in (MetaMask, Rainbow, Trust Wallet and Coinbase Wallet) or email and password, receiving one dedicated personal EVM deposit address (0x…) valid across all six supported chains. Confirmed USDC and USDT transfers credit at exact 1:1 USD parity with zero swap spread, $0 deposit fees, $0 idle management fees, and $0 platform withdrawal fees from a $10 USD minimum on Available Balance.

Before committing live capital, prospective users can test the complete staking workflow through a prefunded $10,000 virtual USDC Demo Sandbox simulating 30-day stake cycles, 24-hour accruals, and compounding controls in full isolation from live protocol balances.

Each 30-day stake accrues on an independent rolling 24-hour clock from activation within a variable applied daily yield band of 0.10% to 0.30% per day. Rather than charging fees on principal, Butter Capital deducts a flat 5.00% Cortex Performance Fee strictly from positive gross daily yield, waived to 0.00% across the entire first 30-day cycle for users joining via a partner or referral link.

Participants can adjust a live 0% to 100% Auto Compound setting on any active stake mid-cycle to either reinvest daily net USD credits into active principal or route payouts directly to their liquid Available Balance, alongside an optional Day 30 Auto Restake toggle. Active staked principal remains committed for its 30-day cycle, while Available Balance funds remain withdrawable on chain 24/7.

Butter Cortex monitors 12 liquid USDT margined perpetual markets, including BTC, ETH, SOL, DOT, OP, LINK, AVAX, SUI, LTC and NEAR, across five quantitative layers. Grok and Perplexity track live market catalysts and funding extremes; OpenAI, DeepSeek and Gemini score L2 order book depth and basis skew; Claude and Llama stress test correlation risk and enforce deterministic invalidation stops; and Mythos stages execution strictly when at least five of eight models align.

Open intraday positions remain private until session close to prevent front running and stop hunting. Every morning at 00:15 UTC, 100% of closed trades from the prior UTC session, including both profit fills and controlled stop-loss exits, publish at https://www.butter.capital/trades with entry and exit prices, USD spreads, UTC timestamps, quorum tags, and direct TradingView (BINANCE USDT.P) verification links. Internal session returns are accounted separately from customer staking rates, with surplus alpha retained in a reserve buffer.

Butter Capital offers a single-level, two-sided partner program from a $10 stake: referrers earn a 3.00% instant USD commission into Available Balance on eligible manual stakes plus an ongoing 5.00% share of invitees’ daily staking yield from platform revenue, while invited friends pay 0.00% Cortex Performance Fees across Cycle 1.

Public web servers derive user deposit addresses exclusively from watch-only extended public keys (xpub) without holding private mnemonics or signing keys, paired with optional TOTP authenticator 2FA withdrawal gates, 24-hour email change cooldowns, and mandatory 48-hour withdrawal freezes after any 2FA reset. Documentation and Whitepaper v1.3 are live at https://www.butter.capital/docs.

Butter Capital, operated by The Butter Capital Limited (British Virgin Islands, LEI 98450051EB909E909F56), is a managed 30-day USD stablecoin staking platform powered by Butter Cortex, an eight-model crypto perpetuals consensus desk publishing closed trading sessions daily at 00:15 UTC. The platform supports 1:1 USDC and USDT deposits and withdrawals from $10 across Base, Arbitrum, BNB Chain, Polygon, Optimism, and Ethereum with $0 platform fees.

PANAMA CITY, November 19, 2026 – BingX, the world’s leading multi-asset trading platform, today announced that Anthropic (Nasdaq: ANT) is available for trading on its platform following the company’s highly anticipated initial public offering. Eligible users can trade ANT/USDT on BingX Spot or take long and short positions through USDT-margined ANTUS(ANTHROPIC) Perpetual Futures. The launch extends BingX’s Anthropic trading offering from pre-IPO exposure to markets linked to its publicly listed stock.

Anthropic’s public listing marks a major milestone for the developer of Claude and brings one of the world’s most closely watched AI companies to public markets. The company set an initial offering price range of $70 to $78 per share, implying a fully diluted valuation of up to approximately $1.4 trillion.

With ANT, BingX further expands its multi-asset offering, which includes more than 700 TradFi instruments through its perpetual futures markets, spanning stocks, indices, and commodities. ANTUS(ANTHROPIC) Perpetual Futures are available for trading 24/7, while ANT/USDT joins BingX’s U.S. stock spot offering of more than 7,000 listings, with 24/5 trading and a minimum purchase of $5. Users can access these markets alongside cryptocurrencies through a single BingX account, without needing to open a separate brokerage account.

BingX introduced Anthropic pre-IPO perpetual futures on May 25, giving eligible users a way to trade changes in its implied valuation before the public listing. The offering was part of BingX’s broader pre-IPO trading lineup, which has also featured companies such as SpaceX and OpenAI.

“An IPO changes where a company trades, but our users’ interest often starts well before listing day,” said Kevin Lee, Chief Strategy Officer of BingX. “With Anthropic, users could trade changes in its implied valuation ahead of the IPO. Connecting opportunities across markets and across different stages of a company’s journey is an important part of how we’re building the BingX multi-asset ecosystem.”

To mark the launch, BingX is introducing the Anthropic IPO Trading Contest, offering eligible participants the opportunity to compete for a total of up to 150,000 USDT in individual rewards. Rankings will be determined by eligible contract trading volume, giving participants an additional opportunity to engage with the newly listed asset.

With perpetual futures, TradFi offerings, spot trading and copy trading, alongside AI-powered innovations, BingX delivers a reliable, intelligent, and responsive trading experience designed to help traders navigate evolving markets and act on opportunities with greater confidence and efficiency.

#5046. Cardano (ADA) Hits Major Death Cross, Will 2026 Year-End Prices Surprise?

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Cardano just saw a death cross on its daily chart, with the 50 MA falling below the 200 MA. The timing of this signal remains significant, as it marks the second consecutive November in the last two years that Cardano has marked a death cross. Cardano saw a death cross in November 2025, and the same is happening this November.

The signal in November 2025 was followed by months of falling prices, which has led some to speculate that the cryptocurrency may have further downside or a recovery ahead of the end of 2026. The current uncertainty in the market has added to concerns. A volatile weekend saw Cardano fall as low as $0.134, its weakest level since December 2020, before rebounding. This recovery has entered its third day, with ADA up 2.05% in the last 24 hours to $0.146.

Whether that rebound holds is the test traders will keep an eye on. The $0.18 area represents an initial resistance level, while a stronger recovery toward the $0.198 to $0.20 range would bring Cardano closer to its daily moving averages.

Another possibility is sideways trading as ADA attempts to establish a sustainable bottom. Its daily RSI is flirting with oversold territory that may trigger a relief bounce, but that’s not enough to confirm a trend reversal. In a bearish scenario, continued selling pressure could push ADA below its recent $0.134 support area, exposing lower price levels.

Crypto markets head into a holiday-shortened U.S. week with inflation data that could shape how big a rate increase the Federal Reserve will consider next month. The personal consumption expenditures (PCE) price index, the Fed’s preferred inflation gauge, is due Wednesday and is expected to show prices rose 0.3% in October after a 0.5% gain in September.

Five different node implementations have now produced blocks on Cardano mainnet. Protocol version 12 testing continues on Musashi and Dijkstra (11.2.1 Pre-release) testnets.

Work progresses on a node version 11.2.x with Mainnet clearance; until then, 11.1.3 remains the recommended version. Node 11.2.0 introduced a new “envelope” config file format, which will be the format published on the config file pages for networks running 11.2.0 and above.

#5045. Bitcoin drops to $55K after $670M liquidations – What changes now?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Last week, Bitcoin [BTC] fell to a low of $55,130 on Saturday, the 20th of November. This was lower than $57,800, which the leading crypto registered on the 1st of July.

It led to debate around whether the bear market was truly over, or whether what we saw in August-September was only a pause within the longer-term bearish trend.

Over the past two weeks, Bitcoin liquidations exceeded $600 million on four separate days.

The 11th and the 20th of November, when BTC breached key support levels at $73k and $61.4k, saw the heaviest long liquidations. Also, contextually speaking, these two days witnessed $690 million and $670 million in long liquidations, respectively.

The persistent downward price action was accompanied by relentless selling. Since the 5th of November, every trading day bar one has seen negative spot ETF flows.

Last week’s net outflows alone reached $1.245 billion. This incessant selling would need to ease before Bitcoin could find room to recover.

As things stand, a weekly trading session close below $57,800 is needed to flip the structure bearishly. The previous week’s correction, though deep, might be just a liquidity sweep before a recovery.

Bitcoin holders are under severe strain as prices approach or drop below their cost basis, and Open Interest has plunged dramatically. It was a result of tightening monetary policy and the global equity market downturn.

Over the past year, the BTC Open Interest was down by $4.5 billion.

In July, this figure fell as low as $6.5 billion. It was the largest OI downturn in Bitcoin’s history in dollar terms, observed crypto analyst Darkfost.

There have been eight trading days in 2026 with over $1.5 billion in liquidations across the crypto market. A decline in risk appetite among investors, several liquidation cascades, and the overarching bearish price action for most of the year helped explain the OI drop.

A leverage reset can be healthy for the market.

Alongside the weekly BTC chart, there was hope for a bear market bottom. Even so, it remains risky to buy, as a bear market continuation cannot yet be fully ruled out.

#5044. Banks Are Adopting Stablecoins: Here Is Why Crypto-Native Dollars Still Win on User Freedom

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

As traditional giants rush into digital dollars, USDD’s Yvonne Chia explains why 24/7 access, self-custody, and transparent yield give decentralized stablecoins an edge banks can’t replicate.

20 November – Road Town, Totorla, British Virgin Islands – Decentralized stablecoin protocol USDD is accelerating its multichain utility across TRON, Ethereum, and BNB Chain—combining automated sUSDD yield via its Smart Allocator with GasFree zero-TRX transfers—as traditional financial giants race to build competing stablecoin rails.

In a recent industry interview with instant exchange partner ChangeNOW, USDD Head of Communications Yvonne Chia outlined why self-custody, 24/7 settlement, and transparent onchain reserves give decentralized digital dollars a structural advantage over bank-issued alternatives.

For billions of people globally, opening and maintaining a traditional bank account still involves paperwork, minimum balance requirements, geographic restrictions, and approval delays. Even when banks integrate stablecoin rails behind the scenes, those services remain gated behind legacy compliance walls and intermediary control.

Crypto-native stablecoins flip that model on its head. As Yvonne Chia highlighted during the interview:

“Compare this to a stablecoin like USDD, where all you need is a phone, a working internet connection, you can immediately start a wallet and you have access to your funds right away.”

With USDD, users can access and manage their digital dollars directly through their own wallets, without depending on traditional financial intermediaries.

This represents a fundamental shift in financial access: users are no longer limited to the services, operating hours, or account structures of a single institution. Instead, they have the freedom to hold, manage, and transfer their assets on their own terms.

Self-custody also gives users greater autonomy over their financial decisions. They can choose which wallets to use, where to transfer their assets, and which supported decentralized applications to interact with, without needing to route every transaction through a centralized financial institution.

With USDD, users’ digital dollars can move with them across an open blockchain ecosystem, rather than remaining tied to a particular bank or service provider.

Legacy payment networks still operate on decades-old schedules: weekend cutoffs, bank holidays, and multi-day cross-border settlement delays accompanied by hefty wire fees.

Blockchain networks never close. Whether it is a Sunday night or a public holiday, USDD moves globally without depending on traditional banking operating hours or settlement windows.

“We are able to have transfers 24/7 and we’re not limited to any traditional banking hours. So I think that in some ways, stablecoins are not just becoming banks, but exceeding what traditional banks can offer to users.”

To make everyday transfers even smoother than fintech apps, USDD introduced GasFree integration on TRON, allowing users on supported wallets to pay network fees directly in USDD without needing to hold TRX.

This removes another practical barrier to stablecoin adoption: having to acquire and maintain a separate gas token just to transfer digital dollars. With GasFree, eligible users can pay approximately 0.5 USDD per transaction, compared with around 1.5 USDT for a standard USDT transfer under the referenced fee conditions.

These improvements matter because the usefulness of a stablecoin is not determined by its peg alone. Low-cost transfers, straightforward wallet experiences, and around-the-clock availability are what make digital dollars practical for everyday payments and global value transfers.

Perhaps the sharpest contrast between banks and decentralized stablecoins lies in how yield is distributed.

In traditional banking, institutions take customer deposits, deploy them into yield-generating markets, and retain a significant portion of the returns. Everyday depositors may receive relatively low interest rates, while higher-yielding products often come with eligibility requirements, minimum deposits, or fixed maturity periods.

Stablecoins introduce another way to participate in digital-dollar opportunities. Rather than keeping capital entirely within a traditional deposit account, users can access onchain yield opportunities through decentralized protocols and transparent investment mechanisms.

USDD was built to return that value directly to holders. Through USDD Earn, users can deposit USDD to receive sUSDD, earning automated, competitive yield powered by our Smart Allocator system, with no fixed lock-up periods restricting access to their capital.

For users, the distinction is important: USDD Earn provides access to a productive stablecoin while maintaining flexibility, rather than requiring users to commit their capital to a conventional fixed-term product.

Traditional banks ask customers to trust closed-door balance sheets and periodic regulatory filings—until a crisis proves otherwise.

USDD takes a different approach through its “Don’t trust, verify” ethos, emphasizing publicly accessible onchain information and independent verification.

Transparency does not eliminate financial or technical risks, but it changes how users can evaluate them. Instead of relying exclusively on an institution’s assurances, users have access to information that can be independently examined and verified.

The bigger picture is an interconnected financial ecosystem in which users can move between holding, transferring, exchanging, and deploying their stablecoins according to their own needs. These are not isolated functions. Together, they make stablecoins more useful as everyday financial infrastructure.

Banks will undoubtedly continue using stablecoin rails to modernize their back-end settlement. However, true financial freedom comes from open networks where assets flow freely across protocols without intermediaries.

For USDD, the opportunity goes beyond offering a dollar-pegged asset. It is about making digital dollars accessible, transferable, and productive across an interconnected financial ecosystem. From self-custody and 24/7 transfers to flexible yield opportunities and multichain DeFi access, these capabilities give users more ways to put their money to work.

Banks may be bringing stablecoins into traditional finance. Crypto-native protocols are showing what stablecoins can make possible beyond it.

Watch the full interview at https://x.com/USDDecentralize/status/2121126175099773054.

Although blockchain trackers recently identified multiple large Ethereum withdrawals by the Binance hacker, it appears that the moves did not materialize into increased Ethereum outflows.

Apart from the attackers' withdrawals, it appears that Ethereum holders on the exchange did not respond to the attack in the conventional way, as security incidents like this often make investors more cautious and wary about keeping their assets on the exchange.

While this typically prompts them to transfer the assets into private wallets where they have greater control, it appears that Ethereum holders remained comfortable with keeping their funds on the exchange.

#5041. Mysterious XRP Ledger Metric Spikes 3,062.1%: What Does This Function Do?

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

One rather unusual metric on XRP Ledger has seen more than a 3,062% increase in usage which raises a lot of questions. This is one of the largest spikes throughout the year in a function usage that mostly stays low.

The function allows users to manage their secondary keys for signing transactions on their wallets and/or accounts. Their main private keys stay offline, while a certain key issued by them is used in daily transactions.

It allows safer work and utilization of their wallets without potentially exposing their private keys. So even if someone grabs hold of your signing credentials, you'll be able to protect your funds by nullifying the issued keys, provided you still retain authorized access.

One way or another, the surge is bizarre. Most likely "legit" scenarios are: automated key rotations among large custodians like exchanges. Some security warnings that triggered massive migration and then, the illicit one: generation of function calls from bots or automated scripts.

The flatlined chart actually represents tens or hundreds of calls daily, but a surge to nearly 4,000 is certainly out of the blue. Unfortunately, we were unable to identify the source of those transactions.

The feature itself is legitimate, so the spike alone does not indicate that users' funds or wallets are at risk. However, an undisclosed exploit or security issue cannot be ruled out without further investigation.

One concerning scenario would involve malicious actors gaining access to wallets and assigning new regular keys to maintain control over them. This would not necessarily mean that the XRP Ledger itself has been compromised, but rather that individual accounts may have been targeted.

For now, there is no evidence pointing toward such an attack, and the activity could just as easily be attributed to routine security operations.

Still, XRP holders should keep an eye on any unexpected changes to their account settings, especially regular keys they did not authorize.

Whether this was a coordinated security upgrade, automated activity, or something more concerning remains unclear. What is certain is that such an unusual spike deserves closer attention, particularly if similar activity continues over the coming days.

#5038. Shiba Inu Dev Flags Security Concerns as Pump.Fun Migration Asks Users for Private Keys

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Long-time Shiba Inu community member and SHIB developer, Mazrael, has reacted to Solana-based memecoin platform Pump.fun's recent wallet login changes, which are sparking concerns about private key security.

In a new announcement, Pump.fun stated that external wallet sign-in is being retired on November 30, 2026. Starting November 30, 2026 (15:00 UTC), Pump.fun on the web will only allow users to sign in with an email account or a Google, Apple, or GitHub account instead of an external wallet such as Phantom, Solflare, or Ledger.

Pump.fun reassures users that nothing will happen to their funds or account, as their wallet address, positions, callouts, followers and username remain unchanged. The only change is how users sign in.

Users who sign in to their accounts with Phantom or any other wallet are urged to take action before the November 30, 2026 deadline.

Pump.fun outlined a number of steps for users on how to switch their sign-in; of particular concern is the step that asked users to enter their wallet details and paste their private key when prompted. Pump.fun noted that this step encrypts the key in the user's browser and that the key is end-to-end encrypted by its login provider, Privy, allowing users' wallets to continue working for everything else.

Mazrael reacted to the development, drawing attention to the potential security implications for users.

pumpfun is retiring external wallet logins on web starting Nov 30. From then on you sign in with Google, Apple, email or GitHub. To keep your existing account..... they want you to paste your private key into their Privy flow 😬 Also note their own terms says they won’t ask for private keys...

The standard security practice across the cryptocurrency industry is that exchanges, wallet providers and other platforms do not ask users to disclose their private keys or recovery phrases. Such warnings are routinely issued to protect users from unauthorized access to their funds.

The Pump.fun wallet login change process appears to contradict this widely promoted security principle, Mazrael noted.

Private keys are highly sensitive credentials that give holders direct access to their cryptocurrency wallets. Whoever gets their hands on them might be able to access those wallets and move the funds. In general, it is recommended never to share private keys with individuals, support agents or third-party services.

#5037. Fusionist ACE Bridge Exploit: $5.7M Loss Estimate, Bithumb Freezes Trading

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

The Fusionist ACE bridge exploit has produced an estimated $5.7 million in losses across two stages, according to SlowMist’s incident tracker. After unbacked ACE tokens were minted on BNB Chain and sold, traders reportedly used the resulting price collapse to withdraw real ACE from the project’s Endurance bridge reserves. The fallout reached centralized exchanges on Monday, November 23: Bithumb halted ACE trading after finding indications that abnormally issued tokens had entered crypto exchanges.

This was not one attacker walking away with $5.7 million in a single transaction. The distinction matters. Roughly $1.87 million was traced through the attacker’s proceeds, while another 53.7 million genuine ACE tokens, valued around $3.7 million at the incident’s reference prices, were reportedly withdrawn through the bridge. SlowMist lists a combined $5.7 million loss estimate, not an audited final total. The bridge’s most basic promise, that a token issued on one chain corresponds to something real on another, is precisely what now needs proving.

Security firm Blockaid flagged the Fusionist bridge-minter exploit on November 22. Its alert described the mechanism bluntly:

A privileged token-bridge minter minted unbacked ACE and sold it for USDT.

The firm initially estimated that about $1.04 million had been extracted while the attack was still ongoing. That was an interim snapshot, not a cap on the damage.

Blockchain-monitoring account Onchain Lens subsequently reported that an attacker had routed 1,091.5 ETH, valued at about $1.87 million at the time, through Tornado Cash. That observation traces a movement of proceeds, not an extra $1.87 million to add on top of the initial extraction estimate. The values describe different points in the same suspected attacker’s fund flow.

Fusionist acknowledged an apparent ongoing ACE exploit on BNB Smart Chain on Sunday and asked users to suspend transfers, trading and bridging on that network. It has not, in the material reviewed, provided a reconciled loss figure or a technical post-mortem identifying how the privileged minting authority was abused.

A bridge minter is supposed to create a token representation only when the corresponding value is properly accounted for. Once the right to mint is misused, a chain can show perfectly ordinary-looking balances that have no matching backing.

It is a familiar failure mode: NewsBTC previously covered a bridge-role exploit that minted unbacked tokens at CrediX. That earlier case does not establish Fusionist’s root cause, but it illustrates why elevated bridge permissions are such a consequential security boundary.

The subsequent drain may prove more important than the initial minting spree.

According to SlowMist’s November 22 incident entry, traders bought the sharply discounted ACE created by the market disruption and used the cross-chain bridge to withdraw about 53.7 million genuine ACE from the Endurance-side reserves. SlowMist valued that withdrawal at approximately $3.7 million.

Here is the mechanism without the jargon. A representation of ACE on BNB Chain suddenly became cheap because unauthorized supply had flooded the market. A bridge that would still accept those tokens in exchange for ACE on Endurance offered an obvious difference in value.

Anyone able to complete that conversion could receive real tokens in return for deeply discounted ones. The trading opportunity existed because the backing and the circulating representation were no longer in a trustworthy relationship.

The $3.7 million figure is a valuation of the ACE reserve outflow, not proof that every recipient sold those tokens for $3.7 million in cash. Nor does it mean every trader involved helped compromise the minter.

There is a meaningful difference between initiating the security breach and trading through a system whose defenses have already failed.

Those distinctions should not obscure the core problem. A bridge can have technically valid transactions and still produce an economically disastrous result. If unbacked supply can be redeemed for genuine reserves, the checks have failed where they matter most.

Bithumb suspended ACE deposits and withdrawals early on November 23. It then halted trading effective 7:30 a.m. Korea time, according to its follow-up ACE security notice.

The exchange said, in a translation from its Korean announcement, that it had:

confirmed indications that abnormally issued ACE had entered cryptocurrency exchanges.

It identified two related BNB Chain transactions and a second transaction.

Those records are important evidence leads, but a full independent decoding of the transaction trail remains outstanding.

Bithumb’s wording is careful: it reported signs of abnormal supply entering exchanges, not a finding that every ACE token in customer accounts is invalid. The trading suspension is a protective response, not a finalized determination of customer losses.

Still, it creates a practical problem for holders who never used the bridge. If tokens are fungible within a market, exchanges and the issuer need a credible method for identifying affected supply and deciding how deposits, balances and trades will be treated.

There is an understandable temptation to focus on the enormous minting figures. The more consequential question is what happened when those tokens met the bridge’s reserves.

A system does not need to lose control of its entire blockchain to suffer a serious failure in one privileged contract role. Nor should a successful-looking bridge transaction count as evidence that the asset being transferred was fully backed.

The response cannot end with a pause notice and a promise to investigate.

Fusionist needs to show which minting permissions were affected, whether they have been revoked, how much ACE was created without backing, how the Endurance-side reserve changed and what claims remain outstanding.

Exchanges need a defensible token-treatment plan, and holders need to know whether there is a shortfall and who is responsible for covering it.

There is a fair counterargument: the $5.7 million figure is an external estimate, market prices moved during the incident, and the precise compromise method is not established.

SlowMist categorizes the incident as private-key leakage, but that classification is not a substitute for an official technical finding. It would also be premature to treat a cautious exchange halt as proof that every affected customer suffered a loss.

Those qualifications change how the numbers should be reported. They do not make the underlying bridge-accounting failure less serious.

The next credible milestone is not a restored interface or a reassuring social post. It is a transparent reconciliation of the tokens minted, the reserves withdrawn and the assets still available to back legitimate ACE claims.

Until that exists, the estimated $5.7 million fallout remains a warning about both token security and the risks of trusting a bridge’s accounting.

#5034. Trump’s Meme Coin Dinner Exposes a 94% Collapse in Holder Commitments

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Donald Trump’s latest meme coin dinner came with a striking financial comparison: wallets displayed on the official $TRUMP dinner leaderboard held approximately $9.1 million in tokens at the November 16 cutoff, compared with roughly $148 million associated with the first dinner in May 2025. That puts the decline in the publicly measured dollar value at close to 94%. It does not mean investors withdrew 94% of their cash, or that 94% fewer people turned up for dinner.

Trump hosted the third event for major $TRUMP holders at his Virginia golf club on Sunday, November 22. The gathering itself was new, but the more interesting story is what the leaderboard reveals about a token that was once able to turn a private invitation into a market-wide buying contest. NewsBTC previously covered an earlier $TRUMP meme coin dinner rally. This time, the numbers tell a considerably less flattering story about the value attached to that privilege.

The roughly $9.1 million estimate comes from Public Citizen’s November 22 review of the 130 wallets displayed on the final public leaderboard. Its researchers counted 9,794,832.87 $TRUMP tokens and valued them at approximately $0.93 each at the November 16, 9 a.m. Eastern cutoff. The older $148 million comparison refers to the first gala’s leaderboard in May 2025.

Source: Official $TRUMP Gala Dinner Leaderboard (November 16, 2026). Rankings based on time-weighted token holdings. Wallet entries do not necessarily represent individual attendees.

That works out to a decline of roughly 93.9%, rounded to 94%. But the events did not have identical leaderboard sizes or necessarily identical valuation methods. More importantly, the token’s price fell dramatically between the two events. A lower marked-to-market balance can reflect falling prices even if some holders retain comparable token quantities.

Calling this a 94% collapse in new investment would therefore be wrong. The narrower conclusion is still hard to ignore: the value represented by the public competition for dinner access is a small fraction of what it was at the first event.

The leaderboard also should not be mistaken for a headcount. While the public display showed 130 wallets, organizers said 185 wallets qualified under the event’s rules. One person can control multiple wallets, and a wallet can be held for an organization. Neither figure identifies exactly who attended Sunday night’s dinner.

The smaller overall value is only part of the picture. Public Citizen found that the top 10 wallets held about 81% of the tokens on the public board. The top 29 accounted for approximately 95%. Those top-ranked wallets were also eligible for a special VIP reception.

The concentration matters because a leaderboard is marketed as a contest between investors, but its aggregate value can be dominated by a very small number of positions. Looking at the $9.1 million as if it represented broad, evenly distributed enthusiasm would be as misleading as treating all 130 wallet entries as separate people.

There is a revealing detail near the bottom of Public Citizen’s review: the wallet ranked No. 130 held only 9.71 $TRUMP, worth about $9 at the snapshot. It qualified because the competition measured time-weighted holdings over the scoring period, rather than the balance left in the wallet at the very end. That is perfectly compatible with the rules. It also shows why a dinner ranking cannot be read as a simple list of fresh purchases or long-term conviction.

There is another market point here. A buyer who wants dinner access can acquire the token and hedge some of the price risk elsewhere. Holding a spot position long enough to earn a place does not necessarily make someone a directional believer in the coin. In that sense, the competition measures an incentive to qualify, not a clean vote of confidence in future returns.

Public Citizen’s researchers traced 66 of the 130 displayed wallets, or 51%, to direct transfers from exchanges that restrict US customers. Those wallets represented about 4.35 million $TRUMP, roughly 44% of the public board’s token holdings. Among the top 29 VIP-ranked wallets, 15 had received tokens directly from such exchanges, according to the group’s November 22 findings.

That is useful on-chain information. It is not proof that 66 dinner guests were foreign nationals, that all those exchanges served prohibited customers, or that any particular attendee broke a rule. An exchange withdrawal identifies a source of tokens, not a passport or the identity of the person controlling the receiving wallet. Public Citizen put a basic transparency problem plainly: “The names of the invitees have not been disclosed.” That sentence matters because a wallet address, however transparent its transaction history, cannot supply the accountability that a named guest list would provide.

GetTrumpMemes.com is not political and has nothing to do with any political campaign or any political office or governmental agency.

That is the organizers’ characterization. It does not remove the transparency question created when holders of a president-linked digital asset can compete for personal access to the president. Neither does that question, by itself, prove an improper exchange of favors.

There is a credible bullish counterargument. An exclusive dinner is a limited, experience driven reward. Its value to attendees need not move in lockstep with the coin’s market capitalization, and falling token prices can make it cheaper for new participants to qualify. The gala went ahead, and its promoters continue to offer other experiences to holders.

But that is precisely why we should be careful about treating an invitation contest as evidence of a durable investment base. Access is an incentive. It is not a claim on business earnings, and it does not put a floor under the price of the coin.

The most uncomfortable part of this comparison for $TRUMP holders is not that the dinner was smaller or less glamorous. The available figures do not establish either claim. It is that the token’s most recognizable promotional feature remains personal access, while the dollar value visible on the public leaderboard has fallen dramatically since the first event.

In our view, that weakens the argument that repeated celebrity events, by themselves, can sustain the market’s appetite for the token. A more persuasive sign of recovering demand would be durable, independently observable holdings beyond a contest period, with less dependence on a few top-ranked wallets. The next useful data would be post-event wallet balances and exchange flows, measured against the November 16 snapshot, not just another photograph from the dinner.

For now, the 94% comparison is a warning about the dollar value of the visible leaderboard, not proof of a 94% investor exodus. That distinction makes the story more credible, and no less troubling for anyone relying on the dinner promotion as an investment thesis.

U.S. President Donald Trump wrapped up his third exclusive dinner for his biggest Official Trump [TRUMP] memecoin holders on the 22nd of November.

The top 185 holders qualified for the dinner, while the top 29 received invitations to a separate VIP reception. In fact, the top four were also promised an 18-karat gold Trump watch. This came after the November midterm elections, where Democrats swept the majority in both the House and Senate.

#5031. Robinhood Chain pays Ethereum 70% more than Base despite collecting 64% less in fees

New data reveals a sharp settlement cost disparity between the two layer-2 networks, even as both retain more than 97% of user fees after Ethereum expenses.

Robinhood Chain paid Ethereum 70% more than Coinbase-backed Base to settle transactions, even though it collected 64% fewer user fees over 30 days.

The network spent approximately $25,700 on Ethereum settlement costs between Oct. 23 and Nov. 21, compared with Base's $15,100, according to Castle Labs data compiled from growthepie.

However, Base generated $2.97 million in transaction fees over the same period, nearly three times Robinhood Chain's $1.07 million.

That disparity left Robinhood Chain spending $2.41 on Ethereum settlement for every $100 collected from users, compared with just 51 cents for Base.

The figures expose a significant difference in the networks' settlement costs, even as both retain the overwhelming majority of transaction fees after paying Ethereum.

The gap is particularly striking because Base consumed considerably more Ethereum data capacity.

According to Blobscan data, Base posted approximately 350,000 blobs during the period, nearly twice Robinhood Chain's 182,000. Blobs let Ethereum's layer-2 networks publish transaction data at lower cost.

Base consequently spent about $4,478 on blob fees, compared with Robinhood Chain's $2,315.

However, the remaining settlement costs tell a different story. Excluding blob fees, Robinhood Chain spent approximately $23,379, more than double Base's $10,602.

Those additional expenses include standard Ethereum transaction fees for posting data and updating settlement states.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Settlement and data-posting costs in USD. Labels show the UTC start dates of four seven-day periods in 2026. The article covers Nov. 20 and 21 separately.

Source: growthepie Robinhood Chain daily settlement fees · growthepie Base daily settlement fees

The comparison highlights a broader imbalance in Ethereum's scaling economy, where layer-2 networks collect transaction fees while paying relatively little to the blockchain securing their operations.

Across all tracked Ethereum layer-2 networks, users paid approximately $5 million in fees during the 30 days ending Nov. 21, while the networks paid Ethereum just $74,500 for settlement and data availability.

That represents roughly 1.5% of user fees, leaving approximately 98.5% after Ethereum settlement expenses, before accounting for other operating and revenue-sharing costs.

Base and Robinhood Chain accounted for about $4.04 million of those combined fees but paid Ethereum only $40,800.

The difference reflects how Ethereum's scaling architecture distributes economic activity. Layer-2 networks handle user transactions and collect their associated fees, while Ethereum provides settlement and data availability for a comparatively small charge.

For Ethereum, the resulting payments contribute to transaction fee burning and validator income, but remain modest relative to the economic activity taking place on its scaling networks.

The November figures raise two questions: whether Robinhood Chain can narrow its settlement-cost disadvantage against Base, and how much additional revenue Ethereum can capture as layer-2 networks continue handling most user activity.

#5030. Shiba Inu (SHIB) Santa Rally Hopes Meet a Grinch: Four Red Decembers in Five

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Shiba Inu (SHIB) gained 5.96% over the past 24 hours and traded at $0.0000038 on Monday, according to CoinMarketCap, as talk of a December "Santa rally" returned to the crypto market. Still, SHIB is down 8.86% over the past seven days, and the token has lost 18.5% in November so far, according to CryptoRank.

Last week, SHIB dropped to $0.00000337, its lowest level since 2021.

The hopes for a Santa rally come after Goldman Sachs analysts said last week that most of the December gains on the stock market usually come after Dec. 15. SHIB's own December history, however, shows that the Santa rally came for the token once in five years.

According to CryptoRank, SHIB lost 29.5% and 13.5% in December of 2021 and 2022, respectively. In December of 2023, the token gained 24.6%. In December of 2024 and 2025, SHIB lost 21% and 17.6%, respectively.

SHIB's median December return is -17.6%, and its average December return is -11.4%, according to CryptoRank data.

The green December of 2023 came after SHIB gained 6.04% in October and 6.62% in November of that year, according to CryptoRank. In 2026, SHIB lost 18.6% in October, and November is also in the red so far.

The Goldman note, titled "GS Flow of Funds: Chopsgiving," came from Gail Hafif, Brian Garrett and Lee Coppersmith, who looked at S&P 500 data going back to 1928. According to the note, the index usually gains 1.1% from mid-November to early December and then gives back about two-thirds of that gain by mid-December, and around 80% of the full 2.4% gain comes after Dec. 14-15.

Since 1990, the S&P 500 has risen after Dec. 15 in 27 of 36 years, according to Goldman.

The Goldman team called these figures a historical map. In the years when the VIX volatility index was above 18 in the middle of November, the S&P 500 gained 1.34% on average, and the index ended the year higher in 59% of cases, according to the note. According to Goldman, the VIX was above that level in mid-November of this year as well.

Goldman also said that retail investors have been buying big tech and chip stocks. Large funds and systematic traders have been cutting their positions, according to the note.

The Stock Trader's Almanac counts the last five trading days of December and the first two trading days of January as the Santa Claus rally. This season, the window will open on Dec. 24 and close on Jan. 5.

In the window that ended on Jan. 5, 2026, the S&P 500 declined 0.11%, according to the Almanac. "Santa was a no-show for the third year in a row," the Almanac wrote. Before that, the S&P 500 also ended the Santa Claus rally windows of 2023 and 2024 lower.

Cardano [ADA] had reached a high of  $0.282 on the 6th of October. Since then, the altcoin has seen a 48.16% price decline.

Cardano market participants’ sentiment has fallen into pessimism, spurred by a nearly 30% price decline for Bitcoin [BTC] and a 36.8% decline for Ethereum [ETH].

In the past 24 hours, Cardano has bounced by 2.66%, and Bitcoin by 1.19%. This was a brief respite from the steady selling pressure, and not a guarantee of recovery.

Last week, the Cardano Treasury Foundation requested $5 million worth of Cardano tokens to host 12 events in 2027, AMBCrypto reported. The recent price drop has presented a slight complication.

#5027. Stellar Adds $207.2 Million Tokenized Fund in Strong Week: What’s Driving Growth?

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Stellar recorded $207.2 million in tokenized funds in the seven days to November 22, reaffirming continued institutional interest in the blockchain.

The RWA Foundation shared data in a post using figures from Token Terminal showing that Stellar ranked among the top blockchains in tokenized fund growth in the seven days from November 15 to November 22, 2026. Stellar recorded $207.2 million: Franklin Templeton added $108.0 million and Spiko added $68.2 million.

.@injective led tokenized fund growth this past week. @StellarOrg had a good week also. https://t.co/7Ip2oK6c1X

This means Stellar continues to grow in tokenized assets, a major highlight of strong growth in Q3, 2026.

Real-world assets tokenized on Stellar amounted to $4.1 billion at the end of Q3 2026, up 36% from Q2 and up 548% from Q3 2025. Two assets drove the bulk of the quarter’s growth: Tradable’s new tokenized private credit and continued inflows into the Spiko Amundi overnight swap fund.

In late August 2026, Stellar held more tokenized non-US government debt than any other blockchain, with about $490 million in sovereign instruments. These included Spiko’s euro Treasury bill fund, Mexican CETES and Brazilian Tesouro bonds through Etherfuse, South Korean Treasury bonds, and a Marshall Islands digital sovereign bond.

Stellar has widened access to tokenized funds in the United States, Europe and Asia. Franklin Templeton’s BENJI has been on Stellar since 2021. In August 2026, the SEC's Division of Investment Management issued a no-action letter confirming that the Franklin Templeton's registered mutual funds and ETFs may use BENJI for cash management. This expands the set of institutions permitted to hold it. Franklin Templeton made a similar move in Europe, registering 'grBenji' as a Luxembourg UCITS share class for third-party distribution, now live on 21X.

In Asia, Marketnode announced in August that it will offer funds from BNY Investments on Stellar, with Marketnode acting as the tokenization and distribution platform. Together, these developments expand access to tokenized funds on Stellar for institutions in the United States, Europe and Asia.

State Street Investment Management and Galaxy brought their Onchain Liquidity Sweep Fund to Stellar in September 2026.

#5026. Solana Price Prediction 2030: Is $2,000 SOL Still on the Table After a 75% Crash?

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Solana has lost roughly 75% of its value since reaching an all-time high of $294 in January 2025. Large futures traders are leaning bearish, yet institutional analysts continue to see a future in which SOL trades at four digit prices. So, how realistic is a $2,000 Solana price prediction for 2030?

At around $73, SOL is trading at a fraction of the price some of Wall Street’s most optimistic forecasts suggest it could eventually reach. But a steep discount to an analyst’s target does not necessarily make an asset cheap.

NewsBTC examined liquidation heatmaps, whale positions, historical price action, network economics and institutional valuation models to understand where Solana stands today and what it could realistically be worth by the end of the decade.

The findings paint a more complicated picture than either bulls or bears might expect.

Perhaps the most interesting signal right now comes from the derivatives market.

According to a November 23 snapshot from PumpGlass, only around 20% of the tracked Binance whale position value was allocated to longs. Meanwhile, approximately 79% of the broader trader accounts were positioned long.

Although the two figures measure different aspects of market positioning, the divergence is striking. Large traders appear considerably less optimistic than the wider trading crowd.

The average entry price of tracked whale shorts stood near $77.63, compared with $77.05 for whale longs. With SOL trading around $73 at the time of observation, the short side was better positioned.

The on-chain picture offers another reason for caution.

On November 19, blockchain tracker Lookonchain reported that two large holders had unstaked a combined 2.31 million SOL worth approximately $169.4 million before transferring the tokens to Coinbase Prime-associated addresses.

Exchange deposits do not necessarily mean those tokens were sold. Institutional investors use custody platforms for several purposes, including collateral management and portfolio rebalancing. Still, the movement is worth monitoring.

What matters now is whether large holders begin accumulating again or whether the recent decline has further to run.

Solana’s liquidation heatmap reveals an interesting battle developing around its current trading range.

PumpGlass identified several concentrations of potentially vulnerable leveraged positions in its November 23, 19:22 UTC observation.

A recovery toward $79 could put considerable pressure on short sellers. Conversely, a breakdown below $69 could expose another wave of leveraged longs to forced closures.

Data from CoinGlass adds another layer to the picture. At 21:31 UTC on November 23, Solana’s futures open interest stood at $3.86 billion, while rolling 24-hour liquidations reached approximately $30.59 million. Shorts accounted for roughly 72% of those liquidations.

SOL had gained around 3.1% over the same rolling period.

The recovery is consistent with some bearish positions being forced out of the market. However, a liquidated short is not the same as a new investor buying SOL for the next four years.

Until stronger spot demand emerges, traders should be careful about interpreting a short squeeze as the beginning of a sustainable recovery.

Liquidation concentrations are modeled estimates, not guaranteed future orders, and can change considerably as traders adjust their positions.

Solana’s price structure has deteriorated considerably since its September recovery.

After closing July near $72.79, SOL climbed to $103 in August and $117.99 in September. The recovery subsequently unraveled, with October closing near $95.35 before prices returned to the low $70s in November.

Looking at SOL’s historical price action on Binance, using the TradingView SOLUSDT market as the chart reference, five key price regions stand out:

The daily Relative Strength Index was in the low 30s in the observed CoinGlass data, suggesting weak momentum and proximity to oversold territory.

But oversold markets can remain oversold.

A sustained recovery above $80 would be the first encouraging development. Reclaiming $95 and subsequently $100 would provide stronger evidence that buyers are regaining control.

On the downside, failure to hold the $65 range would bring Solana’s 2026 low near $60.42 back into focus.

Our technical assessment remains cautious. There is room for a meaningful relief rally, particularly given the nearby short-liquidation exposure, but the broader market structure has yet to establish a convincing reversal.

While Solana’s price has struggled, its blockchain continues to facilitate substantial financial activity.

According to DefiLlama, Solana’s November 23 network metrics included:

There is something particularly interesting about those last two figures.

Applications running on Solana generated considerably more revenue than the chain itself under DefiLlama’s respective measurement definitions.

This highlights what could become Solana’s biggest valuation challenge.

A blockchain can attract substantial activity and billions of dollars in trading volume without its native token capturing a proportional share of that economic value.

Cheap transactions are excellent for adoption. Whether they are equally good for SOL’s valuation is another question entirely.

That is why Solana’s expansion beyond speculative trading could prove so important.

The blockchain’s growing presence in stablecoins, payments and tokenized real-world assets opens the door to financial activity that could persist even when speculative markets cool.

Its infrastructure is also evolving. The Solana Foundation reported the activation of Alpenglow in October 2026, targeting transaction finality near 150 milliseconds. November’s introduction of Solana 1.0 marked another milestone in the network’s development.

Meanwhile, the announced integration with DTCC’s Tokenization Service, expected in the second half of 2027, could represent a meaningful institutional opportunity.

The real test will be how much financial activity these developments ultimately bring onchain, and how much value SOL captures from it.

Institutional research offers a glimpse into how differently analysts view Solana’s future.

VanEck’s October 2023 research produced one of the widest forecast ranges, with its most optimistic scenario placing SOL above $3,200.

Standard Chartered’s Geoffrey Kendrick was reported in February 2026 to have projected a $2,000 Solana price by 2030, partly based on the network’s potential in stablecoin micropayments.

However, the bank’s reported $250 end-of-2026 target looks increasingly difficult to achieve from current levels. Its original research note was not available for independent review.

Perhaps the most interesting analysis comes from Bitwise’s November 2026 research, which connects Solana’s future valuation to tokenized stock trading.

The asset manager’s base case assumes Solana captures 50% of tokenized-stock trading volume in 2031, generating approximately $6.75 billion in annual network economic value.

Applying a 40-times valuation multiple produces an estimated SOL price of $385, based on a projected total supply of 700 million tokens.

The report acknowledges the key dependency:

Most of the price increase depends on how quickly stocks move onchain.

In its bullish scenario, Solana captures 65% of tokenized-stock trading, generating approximately $12.83 billion in network economic value and supporting a modeled price of $1,100.

These assumptions reveal just how much needs to go right before four-digit SOL prices become fundamentally convincing.

Here is something many price predictions overlook: Solana’s token supply will not necessarily remain where it is today.

Unlike Bitcoin, SOL has no fixed maximum supply. Its inflationary issuance framework rewards validators and staking participants, while future governance decisions can influence the rate of supply growth.

With around 592 million SOL currently circulating, we use an illustrative assumption of 690 million total tokens by 2030 for our valuation scenarios.

The numbers put popular price targets into perspective.

These are total-supply valuations rather than projected circulating market capitalizations.

At $2,000, Solana would need to command a valuation of approximately $1.38 trillion under our assumptions.

Using Bitwise’s base-case valuation multiple of 40 times annual network economic value as an illustrative benchmark, Solana would need to generate approximately $34.5 billion in annual economic value to support that figure.

Even at a more generous 60-times multiple, the requirement would be $23 billion annually.

That is substantially more demanding than simply attracting another wave of cryptocurrency traders.

Based on the network’s economic opportunity, competitive landscape, token supply and institutional research, NewsBTC has developed four conditional valuation scenarios.

These ranges are conditional editorial scenarios, not statistically derived forecasts. Outcomes outside these ranges, including substantially greater losses, are possible.

Solana remains relevant but struggles to turn network adoption into meaningful economic value for SOL. Institutional projects develop more slowly than expected, competitors gain market share, and speculative trading remains an important source of demand.

Solana does not need to fail for this scenario to materialize. It only needs to fall short of the expectations investors have placed on it.

Our central expectation assumes Solana retains its position among the leading smart-contract networks while expanding its role in payments, DeFi and tokenized financial assets.

Under this scenario, the network does not need to dominate global finance. It needs to establish a durable position within it and capture more economic value from the applications it supports.

Solana becomes a major platform for tokenized equities, stablecoin settlements and high-frequency financial applications.

Institutional adoption moves beyond announcements, network revenue expands significantly, and the broader cryptocurrency market benefits from favorable liquidity conditions.

At $1,000, SOL would trade at approximately 13.8 times its November 23 reference price.

This is where the most ambitious institutional predictions become conceivable.

Solana would need to capture a significant share of a much larger on-chain financial economy, scale its infrastructure accordingly, and translate that activity into sustained demand for SOL.

An exceptional cryptocurrency bull market could also temporarily push valuations beyond what underlying economics justify.

We would not consider this the most likely outcome based on today’s evidence.

Rather than following every new price target, long-term SOL investors may benefit from monitoring five developments:

Competition from Ethereum, its scaling ecosystem and specialized financial networks remains a considerable risk.

If Solana’s transaction volumes rise while the economic value captured by the network stagnates, we would have reason to lower our valuation assumptions, regardless of how impressive the adoption statistics look.

The current market offers an interesting contradiction.

Solana’s blockchain has established a meaningful financial ecosystem, yet its token is trading roughly 75% below its historical record. Large futures traders are cautious, while institutional research continues to outline substantial long-term opportunities.

Our $180 to $400 base-case range for 2030 reflects the possibility that Solana can grow without fulfilling every ambitious expectation placed on it.

A move toward $1,000 is conceivable under considerably stronger conditions. At $2,000, however, investors would be betting on Solana becoming one of the world’s most valuable digital financial networks.

The opportunity is there. The evidence that would justify such a valuation is not there yet.

For now, the most interesting question surrounding Solana is not how many transactions it can process, but how much those transactions will eventually be worth to SOL holders.

NewsBTC’s analysis places Solana between $180 and $400 by 2030 under our base-case scenario. Strong institutional adoption and improved network economics could support a bullish range of $600 to $1,000, while weaker growth could leave SOL trading between $40 and $110.

Solana could reach $500 by 2030 if its adoption across payments, DeFi and tokenized financial assets continues to expand. Based on an assumed total supply of 690 million SOL, a $500 price would imply a total-supply valuation of approximately $345 billion.

A $1,000 Solana price is possible under a strong bullish scenario. However, achieving and sustaining that valuation would require substantial institutional adoption, higher network economic value and favorable cryptocurrency market conditions. At $1,000, Solana’s implied total-supply valuation would reach approximately $690 billion.

Standard Chartered was reported to have projected a $2,000 SOL price by 2030 in February 2026. However, our analysis considers this an extreme bullish scenario. Reaching $2,000 would imply a total-supply valuation of approximately $1.38 trillion, requiring extraordinary growth in Solana’s financial ecosystem and demand for SOL.

While mathematically possible, a $10,000 SOL price would imply a valuation of approximately $6.9 trillion based on our 2030 supply assumption. There is currently insufficient economic evidence to support such a valuation within this timeframe.

Solana offers long-term exposure to decentralized finance, stablecoin payments and institutional asset tokenization. However, its investment potential depends on whether growing network adoption translates into sustainable demand for SOL. Competition, token inflation, market volatility and regulatory developments remain significant risks.

Solana could challenge Ethereum in areas such as decentralized trading, payments and financial applications. However, overtaking Ethereum by market capitalization would require substantial growth in Solana’s economic value, institutional demand and developer ecosystem. Higher transaction volumes alone would not guarantee that outcome.

VanEck’s October 2023 research placed SOL at $334.70 in its base case and $3,211.28 in its most optimistic scenario for 2030. Standard Chartered was reported to have projected $2,000 in February 2026, while Bitwise’s November 2026 research estimated a base-case price of $385 and a bullish price of $1,100 for 2031.

Weak spot demand, large-holder selling, leveraged liquidations, network disruptions and deteriorating global liquidity could all contribute to further declines. From a technical perspective, losing the $60 to $65 support region identified in our November 23, 2026 analysis would significantly weaken the recovery outlook.

Using the November 23, 2026 reference price of $72.52, a $1,000 investment would purchase approximately 13.79 SOL.

These hypothetical calculations exclude staking rewards, trading fees and taxes. Actual returns may differ substantially, and investors could lose their entire investment.

This analysis uses CoinGlass and PumpGlass market observations dated November 23, 2026, at 21:31 UTC and 19:22 UTC, respectively. Network metrics are provisional DefiLlama observations dated November 23. Technical analysis uses historical Binance SOLUSDT candles. NewsBTC’s valuation scenarios assume 690 million total SOL in 2030 and are conditional editorial estimates rather than statistical forecasts. Third-party forecasts retain their original publication dates and time horizons. Cryptocurrency investments involve significant risk, including loss of principal.
