#4999. Coinbase Targets In-House Prediction Markets by First Half of 2027: Report

Coinbase Targets In-House Prediction Markets by First Half of 2027: Report

Coinbase is planning to launch its own prediction market contracts in the U.S. in the first half of 2027, as it looks to take more control of one of its fastest-growing businesses.

The exchange plans to offer the in-house event contracts to both retail and institutional customers, Bloomberg reported on Thursday, citing a person familiar with the matter.

A Coinbase spokesperson told Bloomberg the company had "nothing new to announce today," and pointed to plans it set out in October to expand its prediction markets and improve their economics by building its own clearing and other infrastructure. The spokesperson did not address the timing of an in-house launch.

Coinbase rolled out prediction markets across the U.S. through a partnership with Kalshi, after unveiling the product alongside stock trading last year. Offering its own contracts would give Coinbase more say over which markets it lists, and let it keep more of the revenue from trades.

The exchange has been laying the groundwork for its pivot. In December 2025, it agreed to buy prediction markets startup The Clearing Company, and in September 2026 it received approval from the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) for its own clearinghouse. Coinbase already runs a regulated derivatives exchange.

Prediction markets have become a bright spot for Coinbase as crypto trading slows. The business passed $200 million in annualized revenue in the third quarter, with daily traders up more than 60% from the previous quarter, and revenue growing 82% a quarter on average since launch, the company reported in October.

"Prediction markets are adding another revenue stream and are less correlated to crypto volatility," CFO Alesia Haas said at the time. On an analyst call the same day, Haas said Coinbase's new clearinghouse meant it "can launch our own contracts, and we can also then earn better economics," while continuing to offer contracts through partners such as Kalshi. Asked when, she said: "We have not shared a timeline."

The products have faced legal challenges from states that argue some event contracts amount to unlicensed gambling. Coinbase sued three states over their attempts to regulate prediction markets, and New York sued Coinbase and Gemini over their offerings in April.

Last week, a Louisiana judge temporarily barred more than 30 prediction market providers, including Coinbase's derivatives arm, from offering event contracts in the state, after Attorney General Liz Murrill sued them on November 12, Forbes reported. The CFTC has sued Louisiana in federal court to block the order, and a hearing on a longer injunction is set for November 30.

Decrypt's parent company, Dastan, operates its own prediction market, Decrypt Predict.

#4997. XRP borrowers face a $207,500 tipping point as one vault dominates an $8 million market

Sentora controls nearly all RLUSD supply in Morpho’s XRP-backed lending market, shaping borrowing costs and credit access.

A single Sentora vault supplied virtually all the Ripple USD (RLUSD) in Morpho’s Ethereum market for loans against wrapped XRP on Nov. 23.

The market showed about 7.1 million RLUSD in outstanding loans and 1 million RLUSD available for new loans. Borrowers use FXRP, a token backed by XRP, to borrow the RLUSD stablecoin.

Sentora’s lending decisions can change both how much credit is available and the interest rate on existing loans.

At about 13:08 UTC, Morpho’s market dashboard put the Sentora RLUSD Main vault’s contribution at about 8.15 million RLUSD, or 99.99% of the market’s supply as displayed.

Borrowers had used 87.45% of the funds supplied to the market, a measure Morpho calls utilization, and the dashboard showed a variable borrow rate of 4.99%.

Earlier on Nov. 23, one wallet posted 3,000 FXRP as collateral and borrowed 1,500 RLUSD in the same transaction, according to Morpho’s indexed activity.

Flare’s FXRP documentation describes FXRP as a tokenized representation of native XRP, and its FAssets system verifies XRP Ledger transactions used to mint FXRP and supports redemption for native XRP.

Flare’s August announcement describes how holders can mint FXRP on Flare, bridge it to Ethereum through the FAssets Portal or Stargate, and post it as collateral in this market. Anyone already holding the market’s FXRP on Ethereum can start at the collateral step.

A borrower’s position can be liquidated if its debt exceeds 77% of the collateral’s value, as measured by the market’s price feed. A drop in collateral value can push a borrower closer to that threshold even without taking out another loan.

In a separate Nov. 23 transaction, the market log shows the Sentora vault withdrew 7,147.88 RLUSD. The vault’s allocation history shows the same transaction moving funds in two other lending markets, one backed by USDe and the other by syrupUSDC.

A later withdrawal of 10,498.74 RLUSD from the FXRP market appears in both logs.

The FXRP market made up just 2.41% of the funds allocated by the Sentora vault, which reported 338.36 million RLUSD in deposits and 95.27 million RLUSD in liquidity across the vault.

In October, the same market had about 8.53 million RLUSD from Sentora and a 10 million RLUSD allocation cap. By Nov. 23, the cap was 15 million RLUSD, while Sentora supplied about 8.15 million.

The available funds for new borrowers were the 1 million RLUSD already in the market. Morpho’s liquidity API showed zero additional RLUSD available at about 13:10 UTC through its Public Allocator, which can move funds from participating vaults when their settings permit it.

Other borrows or allocations can change the amount available before a transaction executes. That same reading showed that about 207,500 RLUSD of additional borrowing would bring the market to 90% utilization with supply unchanged.

Nov. 23 snapshot of Morpho’s Ethereum FXRP/RLUSD market.

New loans could continue above the 90% utilization target while funds remained available. Morpho’s borrowing-rate curve steepens above that point.

Balances and allocation capacity could move before execution. The dashboard and API readings were captured about two minutes apart.

The market bar uses Morpho’s displayed 87.45% utilization as its borrowed share. The available-funds bar enlarges the unborrowed segment. In the zoom, the first 20.3% represents 207,480.83 RLUSD divided by 1,022,276.09 RLUSD from Morpho’s liquidity API. Labels round the underlying values.

Balances and allocation capacity could move before execution. The dashboard and API readings were captured about two minutes apart.

The 90% mark is the reference point for Morpho’s interest-rate model. Borrowing can continue above that level while funds are available, with a steeper rate curve above the target.

If Sentora takes unborrowed RLUSD out of the market while debt stays constant, utilization rises, and the variable rate can climb without anyone taking out a fresh loan. Adding supply with debt unchanged gives new borrowers more room and can ease rates for existing borrowers.

#4995. Bitcoin ETFs Shed $582M in a Day as BTC Drops Below $62K

Bitcoin ETFs Shed $582M in a Day as BTC Drops Below $62K

Bitcoin slid below $62,000 on Friday, extending a weeklong sell-off as investors pulled money from spot Bitcoin ETFs and braced for another interest rate hike from the Federal Reserve.

The leading cryptocurrency was trading at around $61,780, down 2.8% over the past day and 10% over the past week, after touching a 24-hour low of $61,273—its lowest level since early July—according to CoinGecko data. It has fallen more than $2,000 since struggling to hold $64,000 earlier this week, and has also formed a so-called death cross, a bearish chart pattern.

The drop triggered $727 million in liquidations across crypto markets over the past 24 hours, about 86% of which were long positions betting on higher prices, according to CoinGlass. Bitcoin accounted for just under $202 million of the total, with more than 151,000 traders liquidated.

Spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $582.2 million in net outflows on Thursday, their largest single-day outflow since October 30, according to SoSoValue data. The funds have lost more than $1.1 billion so far this week and almost $3.5 billion in November, while their total net assets have fallen to $76.6 billion.

"Within crypto, the most telling sign is this month's outflow from spot Bitcoin ETFs, which suggests investors are choosing to stay on the sidelines for now," Neil Steinhardt, head of Nexo U.S., told Decrypt.

Treasury yields above 5% and expectations that the Fed will raise rates in December have weighed on assets that don't pay interest, including Bitcoin and gold, Steinhardt said. "Because the pressure appears tied to the rate backdrop, and that backdrop has shifted before, a turn in yields or the dollar would likely be the first signal that appetite is returning," he added, pointing to the December 4 jobs report and the Fed's December 9 meeting as the next checkpoints.

Fed Governor Christopher Waller has called for a larger, half-point increase at that meeting, unless policymakers signal another rise in January. Traders are betting on a smaller move: CME FedWatch puts the odds of a quarter-point hike on December 9 at 99.2%, with the chance of a half-point increase falling to almost zero from more than 9% on Thursday morning.

The decline has left the average Bitcoin holder only just in profit. Bitcoin is still trading above its realized price, the average price at which every coin on the network last moved, but the margin is thin, onchain analytics firm Glassnode tweeted on Friday. A break below would put holders in loss for the first time since early 2023, it said.

The average Bitcoin holder is still in profit.

bitcoin:native trades above its realized price, the average cost of every coin on the network. The margin is thin: MVRV sits just above 1.

— glassnode (@glassnode) November 20, 2026

CoinShares head of research James Butterfill sees the slide as part of a longer downturn. "I see this as a continuation of the existing bear market rather than the start of another," he said in a note published on Thursday, pointing to selling by large holders since late 2025, expectations of higher rates and weakness in tech stocks.

CoinShares' base case is for Bitcoin to trade between $60,000 and $90,000 through much of 2027, until falling inflation gives the Fed room to ease, Butterfill said.

#4993. Aave’s USDC loans surge 36% to $2.6 billion as liquid collateral share plunges

Aave weighs a larger USDC bad-debt buffer and lower staking rewards as its loan book grows and withdrawals threaten coverage.

Aave DAO service provider TokenLogic has proposed on Nov. 20 raising the USDC stake target for Umbrella, the protocol’s bad-debt backstop on Ethereum, from 38.84 million to 45.8 million USDC.

The proposal would also cut the extra reward APY at that target from 3.14% to 2.5%.

If holders withdrew from the Nov. 17 cooldown queue at the historical rate with no other changes to stake, about 46 million USDC would remain staked, roughly 240,000 USDC above the proposed target. Under the same test, USDT would fall short.

The proposal would extend Umbrella rewards for USDC, USDT and WETH past their Dec. 2 expiry through April 2, 2027. TokenLogic plans to gather forum feedback before advancing the renewal to a formal Aave Improvement Proposal.

Aave’s ten latest governance proposals showed no corresponding vote or execution as of Nov. 23.

Umbrella stakers put their interest-bearing Aave deposits at risk to cover bad debt. To leave, they first start a 20-day cooldown, then have two days to withdraw.

Across cooldown windows closed through Oct. 26, TokenLogic found that holders withdrew 44% of the USDC and 55% of the USDT they had queued.

On Nov. 17, Holders had staked 53.75 million USDC, with 32.6% in cooldown. At that balance, withdrawals of about 45% from the queue would bring USDC stake down to the proposed target, only slightly above the 44% historically withdrawn.

The USDT vault held nearly 59 million staked, with 55.4% queued. Applying its historical exit share leaves about 41 million USDT, or 91% of its proposed 45.2 million target.

At historical withdrawal rates, the Nov. 17 model leaves USDC 240,000 above target and USDT nearly 4.2 million below.

Source: TokenLogic’s Nov. 20 proposal · Aave governance proposal 507

On Nov. 23, CryptoKeyhole valued the USDC and USDT stakes at about $51.3 million and $46.8 million, respectively, using readings from the Ethereum contracts. Its dashboard reported stake totals without cooldown balances, so withdrawal estimates are tied to Nov. 17.

Active USDC loans on Aave V3 Ethereum Core rose about 36%, from $1.9 billion on June 3 to $2.6 billion on Nov. 17, according to TokenLogic. USDe and sUSDe together rose from 4% to 12% of the collateral backing those loans.

Meanwhile, the share of collateral in the group of highly liquid assets TokenLogic tracks fell from 88.5% to 77.6%.

Against that November loan book, the existing target amounts to 1.5% of USDC borrowing. The proposal would lift that share to 1.77%, below the roughly 2% used in June. TokenLogic also proposed cutting USDT’s extra APY at target from 3.17% to 2.5%.

The 2.50% applies to the additional Umbrella reward at the target stake level. At the higher stake recorded on Nov. 17, the proposed rules would pay about 1.76% in annualized extra rewards.

TokenLogic put the total annualized yield, including deposit interest, at 7% on average over the seven days through Nov. 17.

The Aave DAO takes the first loss up to 1.3 million USDC. Beyond that offset, Umbrella can slash the USDC stake designated for the same Ethereum Core reserve and send the slashed assets to the Aave Collector to cover the deficit.

The Aave DAO’s first-loss offset sits ahead of the matching Umbrella vault on Ethereum Core.

Umbrella vaults absorb deficits in their own reserve.

The sequence shows which funds can cover an Ethereum Core USDC deficit.

The offset comes from TokenLogic’s November proposal and Aave’s earlier governance record. The sequence follows Aave’s Umbrella contract documentation.

The sequence shows which funds can cover an Ethereum Core USDC deficit.

The loss-absorbing buffer comes from what holders stake, and ordinary Aave suppliers draw withdrawals from the lending reserve’s available USDC.

If the renewal takes effect, lending yields and the share of queued stake that leaves will help determine whether Umbrella can sustain the bigger buffer at the lower reward setting.

#4991. South Korea to publish draft of comprehensive crypto legal framework this week

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The draft is expected to include a licensing regime for crypto businesses and a bank-centered issuance system for local stablecoins.

South Korea's Financial Services Commission is preparing to release the government's draft for the Digital Asset Basic Act later this week, according to multiple local news reports.

The act is expected to become the country's second major piece of crypto legislation following the Virtual Asset User Protection Act, which took effect in 2024. While that earlier legislation focused primarily on protecting user assets and preventing unfair market activities, the upcoming bill aims to provide clearer rules for the industry.

Authorities have previously indicated that the Digital Asset Basic Act would cover the legal definition of digital assets and comprehensive rules governing issuance and distribution. The draft is also expected to introduce a licensing regime for crypto businesses, shifting from the current notification system.

A more contested element of the bill is a proposed cap on ownership stakes held by major shareholders of crypto exchanges, widely reported to be in the range of 15% to 20%, to curb concentration risk. The measure has drawn strong opposition from the industry, with critics arguing that it would undermine free-market principles and property rights.

After the FSC releases the draft, it is expected to be merged with proposals from lawmakers and proceed to the National Assembly for further review and discussion.

​​Meanwhile, stablecoins are another major focus of the legislation. This follows South Korean President Lee Jae-myung calling for a local currency-based stablecoin market to protect the nation's monetary sovereignty in the digital age.

The government draft is expected to stipulate a bank-centered consortium model for issuing Korean won-pegged stablecoins, where banks would hold more than 50% in the consortium.

The country's central bank has pushed for this bank-first structure, having previously expressed concerns that unchecked stablecoin issuance could undermine monetary stability. This has also drawn backlash from local industry players who argued that setting this rule would deter innovation.

Other provisions in the bill are expected to further clarify different types of businesses, governance and internal controls of crypto projects, and establish conduct standards comparable to those for traditional financial companies. It would also further strengthen user protection requirements, including rules on asset segregation and operational safety.

#4988. Visa launches experimental AI agent wallet for eligible US users to earn OUSD from paid tasks

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

U.S. users with eligible Visa cards can verify AI agents to access paid assignments on Taskmarket.

Visa Crypto Labs launched an experimental new version of its Visa CLI agent wallet, allowing approved U.S. users with eligible Visa cards to verify AI agents and earn OUSD from paid tasks.

In a statement on Sunday, Cuy Sheffield, Head of Visa Crypto Labs, said users can connect the wallet, add a Visa card on file, and use that credential to verify their agent and register it in Visa's Agent Directory. The wallet is built on Tempo using the Machine Payments Protocol.

Users can earn rewards when agents complete various tasks, such as paid surveys, music composition, market research, and gaming, according to the statement. Taskmarket, one of the platforms Visa is integrating with, allows buyers to post tasks and pay agents in OUSD or USDC on Tempo and Base if their submissions are accepted. Its website showed more than 25 open tasks for verified agents on Monday, with rewards ranging from 10 to 50 OUSD.

Sheffield said the product is intended to help users expand their productive capacity, and not replace them entirely.

"Rather than agents replacing individuals, we believe they extend an individual’s productive capacity to turn their thoughts and ideas into tangible insights and outputs that can create value in the global economy," he wrote.

The launch expands Visa's Trusted Agent Protocol and Agent Directory beyond their earlier applications in e-commerce and business payments, according to the statement.

Visa launched the protocol last October as a tool to help merchants establish whether an AI agent was authorized to transact on behalf of a person or business. Cloudflare, Akamai, and Billtrust were among the first companies to pilot the system.

Sheffield said Visa is extending the same verification tool to agent wallets and task marketplaces, starting with Visa CLI and Taskmarket. Expansion to other platforms is planned for later.

#4987. BTCS sold more Ethereum than it has left to repay $43.8 million in debt

BTCS sells 27,300 Ethereum to repay $43.8 million in Aave debt, halting liquidity pool fees from its largest revenue business.

BTCS sold about 27,300 Ethereum (ETH) on Nov. 20 and used the proceeds to repay $43.8 million of debt on Aave, a decentralized lending protocol, the company disclosed on Nov. 23.

The amount sold exceeded the roughly 24,245 ETH it said it held afterward, based on a preliminary, unaudited estimate.

The sell-off also prompted BTCS’s decentralized finance business, Imperium, to pull its capital out of liquidity pools. That put fees on the withdrawn capital on hold at a business that accounted for most of the company’s third-quarter revenue.

The two decisions were linked by how BTCS financed Imperium. A day before the sale, the company reported about $54 million in Aave debt, and it borrowed stablecoins against some of its Ethereum and used the proceeds primarily to fund Imperium’s liquidity pool positions.

BTCS said it monitors the position and can adjust its borrowing as the value of its ETH collateral changes. A drop in the value of pledged ETH can put that collateral at risk of liquidation.

BTCS Chief Executive Charles Allen said management chose to sell Ethereum and repay Aave to reduce that risk and preserve the company’s ability to meet its obligations on senior secured convertible notes.

“Selling at these prices came at a real cost to the Company and its shareholders.”

BTCS borrowed against ether to fund Imperium’s liquidity pools. The November sell-off prompted a debt repayment and a separate pool withdrawal.

Aave debt fell to about $10.5 million after the repayment, while withdrawn capital stopped earning Imperium pool fees.

Debt balances are approximate point-in-time figures. The post-sale estimate is preliminary and unaudited. The separately reported $43.8 million repayment is not calculated by subtracting those rounded balances. Fees refer only to withdrawn capital.

#4985. Cross River Bank Launches Its First Bitcoin-Backed Loans With LendingUSA

Cross River Bank has launched its first bitcoin-backed loans, working with consumer lender LendingUSA to let eligible borrowers take out between $10,000 and $250,000 against their BTC without selling it.

The program is the first time Cross River has originated bitcoin-secured loans itself, according to The Block. LendingUSA runs the application platform, and every loan is made by Cross River, a New Jersey state-chartered bank, according to LendingUSA.

Borrowers can choose a loan-to-value ratio of 30%, 50% or 70%, which sets how much cash they receive against the value of the bitcoin they pledge. LendingUSA defines the ratio as the loan balance divided by the value of the collateral, so a 50% loan against $100,000 of bitcoin would be $50,000. Two repayment options are offered: interest-only or no monthly payments.

On an interest-only loan, borrowers make smaller monthly payments that roughly cover interest, with the principal and any remaining interest due at the end of the term. On a no-monthly-payment loan, the full balance plus interest is due at the end. LendingUSA says borrowers can repay early without penalties or fees.

Applicants go through a soft credit check, and LendingUSA says approval rests on the crypto being pledged rather than a credit score. The company says funding typically arrives within one to three business days after the collateral is verified.

A qualified third-party custodian holds the bitcoin for the life of the loan. Once the loan is repaid in full, the collateral is released back to the borrower, according to the company.

The terms carry the usual risk of borrowing against a volatile asset. LendingUSA says that if the value of the bitcoin falls, the loan-to-value ratio rises, and borrowers may be asked to add collateral or pay down the debt to avoid liquidation. The company says it will try to notify borrowers first but cannot guarantee it during fast price moves.

The timing matters. Bitcoin was trading near $61,000 on Monday morning, so a borrower at the 70% tier would have far less room before a collateral call than one at the 30% tier. Lower ratios leave a bigger cushion but unlock less cash per bitcoin pledged.

LendingUSA markets the loans for uses such as debt consolidation, home improvements and large purchases. The company says it has served more than 200,000 borrowers across its lending business, and the crypto product is its first expansion into digital asset-backed loans.

Cross River President Evan Russo framed the launch as a natural extension of the bank’s work. “Digital assets have become a meaningful part of how many consumers build and hold wealth, and the financial system must evolve to serve them responsibly,” Russo said in a statement.

“Cross River was built on the belief that innovation and trusted infrastructure go hand in hand,” he added. “Launching our first Bitcoin-secured lending program as the loan originator is a natural extension of that mission.”

The move adds a state-chartered bank to a lending market that has been moving closer to traditional finance. In August, Coinbase and Better Mortgage announced general availability of bitcoin-backed mortgages, and in April, Strike’s Jack Mallers announced lending proof-of-reserves and loans designed to withstand volatility.

Neither company has said how much bitcoin has been pledged so far or which custodian holds the collateral. LendingUSA’s page says rates and terms vary by applicant, and that loan amounts, interest rates and terms are disclosed once an application is received.

For now, the page lists the product’s maximum loan-to-value at 70%, and every loan on it is made by Cross River Bank.

#4982. MoonPay Applies for National Trust Bank Charter to Grow Custody and Stablecoin Business

MoonPay Applies for National Trust Bank Charter to Grow Custody and Stablecoin Business

Crypto payments firm MoonPay has applied for a U.S. national bank charter, joining a growing list of crypto companies seeking a federal banking license.

The proposed MoonPay National Trust Bank would be based in Miami Beach, Florida, according to the company's November 17 application to the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC), Bloomberg reported on Thursday. The charter would help MoonPay grow its custody business and expand its stablecoin services, according to the application.

MoonPay confirmed the move on Thursday, tweeting that it was "proud to share" that it had applied for a U.S. national bank charter with the OCC.

today we are proud to share that MoonPay has applied for a US National Bank Charter with the OCC 🇺🇸 pic.twitter.com/XGHBPx84v0

Unlike a full-service bank, a national trust bank can hold and manage assets for clients, but can't take deposits or lend money. The OCC did not immediately respond to Bloomberg's request for comment.

Founded in 2019, MoonPay is best known for providing on- and off-ramps between fiat currencies and crypto. It has been expanding into new areas including AI, having launched a crypto wallet designed for AI chatbots, let users buy crypto through ChatGPT, and acquired AI trading copilot startup Dawn Labs.

It has also been courting institutions. In April, it acquired Sodot, a Tel Aviv-based crypto key management firm, to form the basis of MoonPay Institutional, a unit serving banks, asset managers and trading firms. The unit is led by former acting Commodity Futures Trading Commission chair Caroline Pham.

MoonPay is the latest crypto firm to seek a federal charter under President Donald Trump's administration. Earlier this month, Coinbase joined Circle and Ripple in receiving final OCC approval for a national trust bank, after Ripple won approval for a trust bank behind its RLUSD stablecoin. Jack Dorsey's Block has also applied for a charter to custody Bitcoin, while World Liberty Financial, the crypto venture tied to the Trump family, won conditional approval for its own trust company in August.

The approvals have drawn pushback. A banking group has sued over Block's application, accusing crypto firms of seeking "side door" access to banking, while Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-MA) has argued that the OCC's approvals of Coinbase and Ripple were illegal, a claim the crypto industry has disputed.

Warren is expected to chair the Senate Banking Committee from January, and is expected to examine World Liberty's charter as part of a wider look at Trump-linked crypto ventures.

#4980. Bitcoin’s next crash may come from holders, not leverage, as $10.7 billion gets liquidated

Futures exposure has contracted sharply, but persistent ETF withdrawals and worsening holder profitability could keep Bitcoin under pressure.

Bitcoin futures open interest fell 12% last week to $29.3 billion, its lowest level since early March, according to Glassnode's Nov. 23 Market Pulse.

Over the same week to Nov. 22, the ratio of short-term-holder supply to long-term-holder supply rose from 15% to 15.5%, its highest since late February. Leveraged traders have thinned out, while recent buyers hold a larger share of the coins, leaving it unclear whether forced selling has ended or shifted to spot holders.

DWF Labs' coin-denominated data showed open interest falling from 712,800 BTC on Nov. 13 to about 639,900 BTC, a 10.2% decline over a slightly different window, which points to genuine position reduction.

Long-side funding payments fell 53.6% to $803,900, so traders pay roughly half as much to hold bullish perpetual positions. Perpetual cumulative volume delta (CVD) moved from negative $1.5 billion to negative $486.5 million, and spot CVD from negative $170.8 million to negative $120.9 million.

Both readings stayed negative, which describes slower net selling.

US dollar value of outstanding futures positions at the two weekly readings in Glassnode's Nov. 23 report. The decline reflects both Bitcoin's price and changes in outstanding contracts.

Source: Glassnode — BTC Market Pulse, Nov. 23

DWF Labs counted over $4.9 billion in crypto-wide liquidations in the week to Nov. 22, including $3.75 billion in longs, about 76%.

The prior week brought nearly $5.8 billion, so roughly $10.7 billion cleared in two weeks. Fewer leveraged positions mean a falling price has less collateral to wipe out. Options traders continue to pay up for protection, as the 25-delta skew rose to 17.4% from 14.4%, its highest since mid-July.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Glassnode's ratio compares short-term-holder supply with long-term-holder supply, a different measure from short-term holders' share of all coins.

The firm classifies entities by average coin age, with a smoothed transition around 155 days, so the ratio can rise as long-term supply falls, as short-term supply grows, or both. A 0.5-point rise is modest, but it arrived while prices fell and futures positions shrank.

Profitable supply fell 6.9%. Both readings come from Glassnode's Nov. 23 report and are rounded to one decimal place.

Source: Glassnode — BTC Market Pulse, Nov. 23

Supply in profit dropped from 54.2% to 47.3%, leaving 52.7% of circulating supply at a loss or near breakeven.

Short-term-holder net unrealized profit and loss sits at negative 18.3%, its lowest since mid-June. Realized capitalization contracted 0.93% over the month, against 0.06% a week earlier, its weakest reading since July.

US spot Bitcoin ETFs lost about $1.7 billion in the week, the heaviest outflow since early July, and Glassnode puts ETF MVRV at 0.97, which places the average ETF holder below estimated cost basis.

Glassnode's Nov. 17 report supplied the $62,000 to $66,000 cluster where many recent buyers entered, the $74,100 short-term-holder cost basis, the $58,500 June low and the $53,700 realized price as key levels.

From a price near $63,000, $74,100 sits 17.6% higher and $53,700 sits 14.8% lower.

If Bitcoin holds the $62,000 to $66,000 cluster while spot CVD turns positive, ETF outflows reverse, and realized capitalization climbs toward zero, the derivatives reset becomes the base for accumulation.

A move toward $74,100 would then test whether recent buyers can reach breakeven and avoid a rush to sell, while futures positions rebuild gradually behind spot buying.

If Bitcoin fails to hold that cluster while ETF outflows continue and realized capitalization keeps contracting, sellers are holders closing losses, and thin futures positioning offers little cushion.

A break below $58,500 would put the $53,700 realized price in view, and both paths are conditional scenarios built on Glassnode's reference levels.

The derivatives half of the reset is complete, while the spot half awaits evidence of buyers large enough to absorb coins that holders sell at a loss.

#4978. ‘The dots aren’t done,’ Michael Saylor says as Strategy buys another 457 BTC for $30 million

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Strategy acquired another 457 BTC for approximately $29.6 million last week, with its total holdings now at 851,305 BTC.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) treasury company Strategy Strategy, Inc. (MSTR) bought another 457 BTC for $29.6 million at an average price of $64,898 per bitcoin between Nov. 16 and Nov. 22, according to a Monday filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission.

The acquisitions increase Strategy's total bitcoin holdings to 851,305 BTC, bought for a total cost of approximately $64.2 billion at an average price of $75,446 per bitcoin, according to the filing. For perspective, that's equivalent to more than 4% of bitcoin's maximum supply cap, but reflects around $12 billion in paper losses at current prices.

The purchases were made using proceeds from at-the-market sales of its Class A common stock, MSTR. Strategy sold 1,925,676 MSTR shares for approximately $176.7 million last week. Approximately $17.9 billion worth of MSTR shares remain available under the ATM program, according to the filing.

Strategy also repurchased 2,183,747 STRC preferred shares between Nov. 16 and Nov. 22 for $211.8 million, using proceeds from its MSTR sales USD Cash reserve. The firm also used $17.6 million from its USD Reserve to pay for dividends on its preferred stocks.

On Friday, during the company's investor Q&A livestream, Strategy co-founder and Executive Chairman Michael Saylor said "natural, organic" demand for the company's STRC preferred stock at its current 12% dividend rate is likely between $5 billion and $7.5 billion.

Saylor described the current environment as a credit-market contraction, adding that the company is responding by reducing the amount of STRC outstanding until it reaches an equilibrium level that the market will support at the existing dividend rate. President and CEO Phong Le said the company has bought back about $3 billion of the preferred stock at prices between $95 and $99, below the $100 issuance price level.

As of Nov. 22, Strategy's USD Reserve and USD Cash balances stood at $3.73 billion and $1.09 billion, respectively, the firm said in the filing, after its board of directors authorized management to reallocate up to $1.0 billion from the USD Reserve to USD Cash.

Bitcoin fell another 6.3% last week, continuing a seven-week slump that saw the largest cryptocurrency drop to its lowest level since September 2024 over the weekend, wicking as low as $55,000 at one point. Bitcoin is currently trading for around $61,400 on Monday.

Despite the continued slide, Saylor posted yet another bitcoin acquisition tracker chart on Sunday stating, "The dots aren't done," indicating the potential acquisition announcement ahead of time.

MSTR fell 30.6% last week, closing on Friday at $77.80, its lowest level since February 2024, according to The Block's Strategy price page.

#4975. Morning Minute: Anthropic IPO Pops, Sinks Markets

Morning Minute: Anthropic IPO Pops, Sinks Markets

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

The largest base IPO deal ever priced at $83 a share Wednesday night, opened Thursday morning at $111.50, and briefly pushed Anthropic’s fully diluted valuation past $2 trillion. The stock finished at $97.17, up 17% from the offer and down 13% from where it opened (it has since fallen about 15% to $88).

A 17% first-day gain on an $81.34 billion raise is a successful listing by any normal standard.

The rest of the market did not fare as well.

The Nasdaq fell 2.06%. The S&P 500 dropped 1.9%. The Dow lost 2.17% and closed below 50,000 for the first time since June. Tech had rallied before the bell on the listing and reversed by the afternoon.

MD fell 7.73%. Nebius dropped 7.8%. CoreWeave lost 5%. Nvidia slid 3.4% despite beating earnings last week. Oracle, Micron, Broadcom and Microsoft all finished lower. The biggest AI listing in history happened, and AI infrastructure was the worst place to be holding.

Joe Maher at Capital Economics put it plainly: “Anthropic’s record-breaking IPO has failed to rekindle enthusiasm for tech stocks.”

Crypto went with it. Bitcoin fell to $61,500, its lowest since early July. That’s a seventh straight weekly loss, which would be the longest run since the nine-week stretch in 2022. Eth hit the $1,750 level, down 28% in thirty days. Gold fell to a fourteen-month low. Silver dropped 3%.

When Bitcoin, equities and gold all sell off on the same session, the common factor is usually the discount rate.

That’s the Fed. Chris Waller spent this week making the case for fifty basis points in December, and the minutes showed he isn’t alone. FxPro’s Alex Kuptsikevich described the market as spooked by “the spectre of a double rate hike” and pointed out Bitcoin is now more than 7% below its 200-week moving average, the deepest it has traded below that line in three years.

So where does this leave us heading into next week?

Start with what an $81 billion IPO actually does to a market. That money came from somewhere. Add the separate $26 billion private placement from Nvidia, Google, AMD and four other suppliers, and roughly $107 billion of capital got committed to a single name in one day. Kuptsikevich raised the possibility that the listing absorbed the riskiest money in the system, and that if so, some of it rotates back out once the allocation settles.

That’s the bull case for crypto over the next few weeks. It requires the Fed to cooperate.

For the bear case, one can simply look at SpaceX.

SpaceX priced at $135 in June, opened at $150, and closed its first day up 19%. Then it kept running, hitting $225.64 four days later, up 67% from the offer. The unwind started the following session. By June 22 it had fallen 16% in a single day and sat 31% off the high. By mid-July it traded below its IPO price. By late July it was down roughly 50% from the peak.

That would project out to a market low around Christmas time, which is actually how the 2018 midterm cycle played out. Will history repeat itself? We will find out soon enough. In the interim, expect volatility.

#4974. Solo bitcoin miner beats the odds to win $191,000 block reward

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The miner reported just 11.1 TH/s on Solo CKPool, with an estimated one-in-1,650 chance of finding a block in a year.

A solo bitcoin miner beat the odds to solve a block carrying nearly $191,000 in rewards on Sunday.

The miner collected a total of 3.129 BTC ($190,764) for mining block 977,057, using solo bitcoin-mining software from CKpool, according to data from Bitcoin explorer Mempool. That comprised the 3.125 BTC block subsidy reward ($190,538) and 0.004 BTC ($226) in transaction fees, before CKpool's 2% fee.

"Congratulations to miner bc1q~zy9j for solving the 323rd block - yet another lucky one at EU solo.ckpool.org with only ~11.1 TH of hashrate on a 3.47 TH worker," CKpool developer Con Kolivas posted on X. "A miner of this size has about a one in 1650 chance of solving a block in a year."

The reported 11.1 TH/s is comparable to the combined output of a few home desktop miners, significantly smaller than top mining machines like Bitmain's U3S23H Antminer, with 1,160 TH/s. In comparison, top public bitcoin miners Bitdeer (BTDR), MARA Holdings (MARA), and Riot (RIOT) manage a hashrate of approximately 82.3 EH/s, 71.2 EH/s, and 37.2 EH/s, respectively, according to Ziven data. Those figures are approximately 7.4 million times, 6.4 million times, and 3.4 million times greater than the solo miner's hashrate.

The solo miner's hashpower is equivalent to about 0.000001% of the Bitcoin network's total estimated hashrate of 1.07 ZH/s on Nov. 23, per Mempool's data, which measures the total computational power dedicated to the network.

Smaller Bitcoin miners usually join shared pools to earn steady, proportional rewards since solo mining has an extremely low chance of winning a block. However, some try solo pools instead for a shot at the full block reward, hoping for a big, lottery-esque win despite the odds.

While CKpool is technically a mining pool, it is not used in the conventional sense where miners combine their hashrate for a better chance at block discovery by collaborating and sharing the rewards. Instead, miners using the solo CKpool work independently, keeping the full block reward if they find one, minus CKpool’s 2% fee. It avoids the need to run your own full node and pool infrastructure, making it a simple setup compared to true solo mining.

However, it's not the first time a solo miner has won Bitcoin blocks.

In April, another miner using CKpool in a solo mining configuration collected roughly $210,000 for mining block 943,411. A miner of that size has roughly a 1-in-28,000 chance of finding a block on any given day, Kolivas said at the time.

In November 2025, another solo miner reporting just 6 TH/s won a block worth around $265,000 at the time.

Solo miners with more substantial managed or rented hashrates have also found several Bitcoin blocks in recent years.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) is currently trading for around $61,221, up 1.2% over the past 24 hours, per The Block's BTC price page.

#4973. Solo Bitcoin Miner Wins $191,000 Block Reward With Just 11 TH/s

A solo miner running about 11 terahashes per second has mined Bitcoin block 977,057, collecting a reward of 3.1287 BTC, or about $191,000, on a machine that makes up roughly one-millionth of a percent of the network.

The block was found through the solo service at CKpool on Sunday, Nov. 22, at about 7:54 a.m. ET. The reward is the 3.125 BTC subsidy plus about 0.0037 BTC in transaction fees, and it was worth roughly $191,000 at bitcoin’s price when the block landed.

The block holds 584 transactions. Its coinbase transaction pays 3.0661 BTC to a single address and 0.0626 BTC, about 2% of the reward, to a second output. That split matches the service’s 2% fee, which means the miner’s take is a little over $186,000.

Con Kolivas, the developer behind CKpool, congratulated the miner on X, whose address he abbreviated as bc1q~zy9j. It matches the end of the address that received the payout. Kolivas called it the 323rd block found on the service and “yet another lucky one.”

He said the miner had only about 11.1 TH/s of hashrate on a 3.47 TH worker. “A miner of this size has about a one in 1650 chance of solving a block in a year,” Kolivas wrote.

The block was tagged to Solo CK on the mempool.space block explorer, and its coinbase carries the pool’s signature. CKpool describes itself as a service that lets miners go after full solo blocks without running their own node, since you cannot mine directly to a Bitcoin Core node.

Solo CK has found five blocks since Nov. 6, according to the explorer’s pool page. The most recent before this one was block 976,642 on Nov. 19, followed by blocks on Nov. 11, Nov. 7 and Nov. 6. Each paid between 3.13 and 3.15 BTC.

Earlier this year, a different solo miner won block 943,411 on the same service on April 2, for about 3.139 BTC. In October, a FutureBit Apollo solo miner found block 973,262 and earned 3.154 BTC.

Those wins are the exception. Most of the network’s hashrate sits in large pools and industrial operations that split rewards across many miners, which is how they smooth out the luck. A single small machine can run for years without finding anything, and a lucky block can show up on day one. Miners on the service get nothing unless they find a block, and then they keep the full reward minus the 2% fee.

Bitcoin’s network hashrate was about 1.04 zettahashes per second, or roughly 1,043 exahashes, on a three-day average Monday. At 11.1 TH/s, the winning miner contributes about 0.000001% of that total, which fits Kolivas’s odds. One in 1,650 works out to a chance of about 0.06% over a full year of mining.

Mining difficulty is about 134.8 trillion. The last adjustment cut it by about 9.1%, and the next one, expected around Nov. 26, is estimated to raise it by about 9.2%, according to mempool.space. Difficulty resets every 2,016 blocks to keep blocks arriving about every 10 minutes.

Mining conditions have been tough. Bitcoin plunged to about $55,100 over the weekend before rebounding to near $64,000 on Monday morning, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro. Lower prices cut the dollar value of every block, whether it goes to an industrial miner or a hobbyist running a small solo rig.

The subsidy is fixed at 3.125 BTC until the next halving, so what the solo winner earns depends on fees and the market. A 3.1287 BTC block was worth about $196,000 at Monday’s price near $62,700, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $62,700.

#4970. Pump.fun draws criticism over private key imports as it retires external wallet sign-in on web

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Former Mt. Gox CEO Mark Karpelès questioned Pump.fun’s description of the process as end-to-end encrypted.

Solana-based trading platform Pump.fun’s plan to end external wallet sign-in on the web has drawn criticism over its request that users import private keys to keep their existing wallet-linked accounts.

Users can sign in with an email address or opt for social logins through Google, Apple, or GitHub instead of connecting an external wallet such as Phantom, Solflare, or Ledger, according to a statement.

After Nov. 30, users who access Pump.fun through an existing wallet session will be prompted to add an email or social login, the platform said, adding that their accounts and funds will remain intact.

Pump.fun said private keys imported during the migration process are encrypted in the browser and held by its login provider, Privy.

Meanwhile, Google sign-in will not work inside wallet browsers, including Phantom's in-app browser, and users might need to sign in with email or switch to Safari or Chrome, Pump.fun said.

Former Mt. Gox CEO Mark Karpelès objected to the migration in posts on Sunday and Monday. According to his analysis, Privy’s infrastructure decrypts and holds imported keys, which he argued makes Pump.fun’s description of the process misleading.

"Calling this 'end-to-end encryption' is misleading at best," Karpelès wrote in a post. "End-to-end encryption means only you can decrypt. Here the encryption does happen in your browser, but the decryption key belongs to Privy: their infrastructure (a TEE) decrypts your private key, keeps it, and signs with it for whoever passes their login check "

Karpelès argued that requiring users to copy and paste their private keys was illogical because the platform already uses standard "Sign in with Solana" cryptographic messages to verify users.

The migration affects only web sign-in. It does not alter users’ wallet addresses, positions, callouts, followers, or usernames, according to Pump.fun. The mobile app, API sessions, and trading are unaffected.

The Block reached out to Pump.fun and Privy for comment.

#4969. Senate Passes Bill Banning Prediction Market Bets on War, Terrorism and Death

Senate Passes Bill Banning Prediction Market Bets on War, Terrorism and Death

The Senate has passed a bill that would ban prediction markets from offering bets on war, terrorism, assassinations and deaths, sending the measure to the House of Representatives.

The Discouraging Exploitative Assassination, Tragedy, and Harm Betting in Event Trading Systems Act, or DEATH BETS Act, passed the Senate by voice vote on Wednesday, November 19, with an amendment, according to Congress.gov. The bill was introduced by Sen. Adam Schiff (D-CA) in March, and is cosponsored by Sen. John Curtis (R-UT).

"With this bill, Congress is making it clear that these bets should be illegal," Schiff tweeted after the vote.

BREAKING: The Senate just passed my bill with @SenJohnCurtis to prohibit prediction markets from listing contracts involving terrorism, assassination, war, military operations, or an individual’s death.

With this bill, Congress is making it clear that these bets should be… pic.twitter.com/Mzu5PWmGsL

— Senator Adam Schiff (@SenAdamSchiff) November 20, 2026

The bill would amend the Commodity Exchange Act to bar exchanges and clearinghouses registered with the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) from listing or clearing event contracts that expressly reference terrorism, assassination, war or U.S. military operations.

It would also ban contracts that, at the time of listing, are "reasonably expected to resolve principally based on a death of an individual." The CFTC would be able to extend the ban to "any similar activity" by rule.

The bill would not affect the status of other types of contracts covered by existing CFTC rules on event contracts, such as those involving gaming, according to its text.

Prediction markets such as Kalshi and Polymarket let users trade on the outcome of real-world events, from elections and sports to geopolitics. Markets on wars and the fates of world leaders have drawn criticism from lawmakers, who have argued they let people profit from tragedy, and could reward insiders with knowledge of military plans. Polymarket users have been accused of insider trading after making $2.4 million on Iran-related predictions. Polymarket pulled a market on whether a nuclear weapon would be detonated after a public backlash.

Senate Democrats have raised concerns that people close to President Donald Trump could profit from war-related markets, and one senator has alleged White House corruption over Iran war predictions. Kalshi was also sued after refusing to pay out on a market tied to the death of Iran's then-Supreme Leader, Ali Khamenei.

The CFTC has also moved to curb such markets. In June, it proposed rules that would ban markets on outcomes that could come about through war or assassination, such as vaguely worded bets on when U.S. adversaries would be "out of office," which Kalshi and Polymarket had used to sidestep existing bans on war-related contracts.

War-related markets have also appeared elsewhere, including on Myriad, the prediction market operated by Decrypt's parent company DASTAN, where, where users traded on whether U.S. troops would enter Iran.

The bill's passage comes as prediction markets face growing legal pressure from states, which argue that some event contracts amount to unlicensed gambling. New York, for example, has sued Coinbase and Gemini over their prediction markets. The CFTC, which first sued three states in April to defend its authority over the markets, has now taken 12 states to court, most recently Louisiana.

#4965. OKXICE launches tokenized securities venue with 113 assets confirmed for 24/7 trading

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The current lineup of issuers and asset managers includes eToro, Fidelity, Strive, BitGo, Circle, Coinbase, Robinhood, and Strategy.

OKXICE, the joint venture between OKX and Intercontinental Exchange, has launched its tokenized securities venue (TSV) for eligible U.S.-based OKX Inc. customers through the OKX app.

The platform launches with 113 assets, including issuer stocks and exchange-traded funds. The current lineup of issuers and asset managers includes eToro, Fidelity, Strive, BitGo, Circle, Coinbase, Robinhood, and Strategy.

Every token traded on the venue represents a security entitlement backed 1-to-1 by an underlying share, and offers applicable shareholder rights and economic benefits. This includes dividends, voting rights, and corporate actions.

"With OKXICE TSV, we're breaking down the barriers of traditional trading and bringing real securities into a 24/7 marketplace — without sacrificing the shareholder rights and protections investors expect," OKXICE co-Chair Andrew Cuomo said in a statement shared with The Block. "We're not just talking about the future of Wall Street. We're building it."

Trading of the tokens is supported by AMM liquidity pools on Uniswap v4 that are built on OKX's Ethereum Layer 2, X Layer, while Securitize provides the tokenization infrastructure. Trading and liquidity provision are limited to eligible wallets by smart contracts, and the tokenized securities remain in customers' self-custodial wallets.

OKXICE TSV operates under the Securities and Exchange Commission's Innovation Exemption, which is a temporary five-year order allowing limited onchain trading of tokenized U.S.-listed stocks without the venues having to register as exchanges.

"We’re grateful to our first-mover issuers for helping us turn this vision into a live market, and we look forward to expanding access as the venue grows,” said Trabue Bland, co-Chair of OKXICE.

Meanwhile, the OKXICE TSV launch was preceded by widespread issuer pushback. According to the venue’s latest public notice, 67 companies have filed a Notice of Issuer Objection, preventing tokenized versions of their shares from being offered on the platform. These include major issuers such as Apple, Microsoft, Alphabet, Nvidia, Amazon, Tesla, Meta, JPMorgan Chase, Goldman Sachs, Walmart, Costco, Boeing, and Netflix, among others.

#4961. Bitdeer Sells All 281 Bitcoin It Mined Last Week, Holds None

Bitdeer sold every bitcoin it mined last week and still holds none of its own, the company said in its latest weekly update, continuing a nearly year-long strategy of turning production into cash after a weekend plunge in the bitcoin price.

The Singapore-based miner reported output of 281.1 BTC and sales of 281.1 BTC for the week, for a net addition of zero. Its pure holdings, which exclude customer deposits, were zero as of Nov. 20.

The sales came as bitcoin fell to a weekend low of $55,106 early Saturday at 1:17 AM ET, Bitcoin Magazine Pro data shows. The price has since bounced to near $62,700 as of 11:23 AM ET Monday.

Bitdeer’s treasury shrank from roughly 2,000 BTC at the end of 2025 to zero by Feb. 20, when Bitcoin Magazine reported the miner had liquidated its reserve over eight weeks. At the time, the company said the move should not be a concern for the broader market.

Bitdeer’s filings show how little it has kept since. In its third-quarter results, filed with the SEC on a Form 6-K on Nov. 10, the company listed 47 BTC held at Sept. 30, down from 2,029 a year earlier.

Its September production update showed 61 BTC held at the end of August. The company mined 1,355 BTC in September and 3,855 BTC in the third quarter, compared with 1,109 BTC in the same quarter of 2025.

Bitdeer has grown its fleet quickly. Self-mining hashrate reached 82.3 exahashes per second in the third quarter, up from 35.0 a year earlier, with another 28.7 exahashes per second of co-mining at third-party sites, the quarterly report shows.

The economics are tight. Self-mining generated $217.5 million in revenue in the quarter but only $6.2 million in gross profit. Electricity averaged $47 per megawatt-hour, and the fleet ran at 15.4 joules per terahash.

Bitdeer posted record revenue of $320.7 million and adjusted EBITDA of $69.6 million, but a net loss of $125.5 million. That included a $54.6 million non-cash loss tied to a bitcoin-collateralized loan from a related party.

Bitdeer also reported $213.7 million in cash and restricted cash, and its board authorized a new $100 million share repurchase program on Oct. 8.

Not every miner is selling. American Bitcoin secured a $150 million revolving credit line from FalconX on Monday that is backed by its bitcoin. The company said the facility lets it raise cash without drawing down its reserve.

The loan carries a 5.95% fee and an initial collateral ratio of 143%. Fully drawing it would require about $214.5 million in pledged bitcoin. American Bitcoin has not said whether it has drawn on the line, and its release lists the risk that pledged coins could be liquidated if prices fall.

American Bitcoin, which is majority-owned by Hut 8, said on its Nov. 4 earnings call that it held 8,427 BTC at the end of September. The company has also sold part of its production to cover operating costs.

Other miners have gone the other way. MARA sold 13,597 bitcoin earlier this month, as miners facing thin margins turned to their treasuries for cash.

Shares of Bitdeer (BTDR) were trading near $3.44 at 11:23 AM ET, up about 16% from Friday’s close of $2.96. The stock has ranged from $3.26 to $3.71 on the day as a broad market rally lifted bitcoin-linked stocks after Nvidia reinstated CEO Jensen Huang.

Bitdeer has not said whether it plans to rebuild a bitcoin position. It said in February that selling was a liquidity measure tied to evaluating powered land acquisitions and scaling its infrastructure.

The company is also shifting toward AI cloud services, and it reported an AI cloud revenue backlog of about $2.9 billion in its third-quarter release.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $62,700.

#4960. Bitcoin Breaks Below $60K as Crypto Fear Index Hits Worst Level Since February

Bitcoin Breaks Below $60K as Crypto Fear Index Hits Worst Level Since February

Bitcoin broke below $60,000 on Friday, plunging through a major psychological price level as the selloff gathered pace after Anthropic’s stock market debut.

The daily chart shows BTC at $59,703.72 , down 3.55% for the session after touching a low of $59,300.

The Fear and Greed Index chart dipped to 9, a reading classified as “extreme fear” and its lowest since February. For reference, the index was at 18 a week ago and a neutral 51 a month earlier.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Bitcoin with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

The indicator tracks crypto market sentiment on a scale from zero, or maximum fear, to 100, or extreme greed, using market data rather than individual investor surveys. Its 2026 low was 5 on February 5, compared with a yearly high of 82 on August 26.

The selling went well beyond Bitcoin. Ethereum fell 4.24%, Solana lost 6.36%, BNB slid 4.38% and XRP declined 5.53% over the same period.

Forced selling added to the pressure. As Decrypt reported earlier Friday, roughly $727 million in leveraged crypto positions had been liquidated over 24 hours, about 86% of them bullish bets, while U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs lost $582.2 million on Thursday.

Spot fund withdrawals did not begin with Anthropic’s IPO. By Thursday, roughly $2.9 billion in U.S. Bitcoin ETF outflows for November, alongside higher Treasury yields and expectations of tighter Federal Reserve policy.

Anthropic made its Nasdaq debut Thursday at $111.50, up 34% from the $83 IPO price, before closing at $97.17. Friday brought another decline for the Claude developer, with the stock almost losing all the prime it gained after its IPO.

The listing raised $81.34 billion through the public offering, but enthusiasm for Anthropic shares did not carry over to the companies building the AI boom.

Friday’s market heatmap shows tech stock suffering in the middle of mostly green pastures. Nvidia down 3.78%, Broadcom is down 5.65%, AMD declining 5.06% and memory-chip maker Micron dropping 6.51%. Meta fell 2.21%, adding to pressure on companies spending heavily on AI infrastructure.

Other major stocks were doing better. Alphabet rose 2.81%, Microsoft gained 0.50% and Apple edged up 0.25%, while healthcare stocks Eli Lilly and Johnson & Johnson rose 1.21% and 1.78%, respectively. Walmart added 2.91% and Visa climbed 1.34%.

But it was not a uniform crash across the S&P 500. The tech-heavy Nasdaq was falling more sharply than the broader index, while pockets of healthcare, consumer, and financial shares remained green.

Bitcoin’s daily chart turned more decisively bearish after the break below $60,000. Its 50-day exponential moving average, or EMA, was below the 200-day EMA, a death-cross configuration, and spot prices were trading beneath both lines. That confirms sustained downside momentum rather than a brief intraday dip.

The relative strength index, or RSI, had fallen into the teens, far below the 30 threshold usually associated with oversold conditions. The average directional index, or ADX, climbed to 43.7, signaling a strong trend, with the chart’s directional readings favoring sellers. Oversold does not mean a bounce is guaranteed.

The longer-range chart puts the $60,000 breakdown in context. Bitcoin traded around this area several times in 2024 and returned to it during the mid-2026 retreat, making it a visible historical price zone as well as a round-number threshold.

With $60,000 giving way, the roughly $58,000–$59,000 support area near the mid-2026 lows comes into focus. If that zone fails, the next notable historical demand area is around $55,000–$56,000, followed by the $50,000 region marked by the deeper 2024 selloff.

For bulls, $60,000 is now the first level to hold. The next hurdle is $62,000–$62,500, followed by $65,000 and heavier resistance near $68,000–$70,000. A brief bounce back above $60,000 would not, by itself, reverse the bearish trend.

As of right now, Bitcoin had touched $59,300.13, confirming that sellers had pushed below the $60,000 support zone. The next test is whether that level becomes resistance on a rebound.

#4958. Bitcoin, crypto and tech stocks rally as Nvidia reinstates Jensen Huang as CEO; NVDA jumps 30%

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Bitcoin climbed back above $63,000 and Nvidia jumped 30% pre-market as broader crypto and tech stocks rallied.

Crypto and tech stocks rallied early Monday as Nvidia's board announced it had reinstated Jensen Huang as president and CEO.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) climbed back above $63,000, up 3.4% for the day as the GMCI 30 index of leading cryptocurrencies saw a 5.5% jump amid broader tech market gains.

Just ahead of the announcement, Polymarket put the odds of Huang rejoining Nvidia by the end of the year at just 10.5%, and 2.5% before the end of this month.

Nvidia (NVDA) climbed more than 30% at one point in pre-market trading to around $164, a move that continued into early morning trading on Monday. The move helps alleviate a torrid trading period for the company, which has seen its stock fall around 43% over the past month amid Huang's resignation on Nov. 12, amid reports that he had been the target of a foreign intelligence effort.

Nvidia had appointed Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress as interim CEO while the board considered internal and external candidates, and as recently as Tuesday's quarterly earnings call, said it did not intend to reinstate Huang despite growing calls for him to return as its stock price plunged.

President Donald Trump was one of Huang's strongest supporters during the period, and said Monday that the board had "made the correct and proper decision" to reinstate him as CEO.

"The Country needs Jensen's leadership at NVIDIA in order to ensure the Success and Prosperity of not only the Company, but the American Economy," President Trump wrote Monday on Truth Social. "The United States Government has no reason to believe that Jensen's return to NVIDIA poses any risk to National Security. The Attorney General of the United States has confirmed that the U.S. Government does not possess evidence that he has engaged in any unlawful conduct."

In a statement on Monday, Nvidia said the reinstatement was effective immediately and was the result of a unanimous decision by the board.

"In the process of reaching its decision, the Board received input from senior levels of the United States government, and based on that input, the Board is confident that Mr. Huang's reinstatement poses no national security risk," it said.

Chair of the board Harvey C. Jones said Huang is the right person to lead Nvidia, and his reinstatement is in the best interests of the company's stockholders, employees, partners, and customers.

“I am honored to return to Nvidia," Huang said. "I take this responsibility with the greatest humility and renewed commitment."

The board confirmed that, in light of reporting that Huang had been the target of a foreign intelligence effort, the independent investigation it had already launched would continue. Huang fully supports the investigation and is committed to cooperating with it, the board said.

#4955. Wrapped Monero Token Collapses 99% After $13 Million Hack Blamed on Web Host

Wrapped Monero Token Collapses 99% After $13 Million Hack Blamed on Web Host

A wrapped version of Monero lost nearly all its value Friday after an attacker drained its deployer wallet and dumped the tokens on Hyperliquid.

XMR1, a token issued by cross-chain swap service Wagyu that represents Monero on Hyperliquid, fell more than 99% in roughly half an hour, from about $500 to around $1.

The account that deployed the token sent 5.2 million XMR1 to a newly created account early Friday, according to an analysis by onchain data firm Bitquery.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Bitcoin with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

That account sold about 364,000 of the tokens to 388 buyers for $13.5 million, then converted the proceeds to ETH. At Bitquery’s last check, about 7,350 ETH sat in three wallets.

Wagyu’s pseudonymous founder, PerpetualCow, blamed a breach at 1984.hosting, an Icelandic web hosting provider.

He wrote on X that the XMR1 deployer key had been stored on an old server there that he had deleted, but that the host apparently kept its logs and files. “Lesson to learn here is that these hosting providers keep your data/logs even after you delete your server with them,” he wrote.

1984 ehf., the company behind the service, said it had suffered an extensive and serious cyberattack on its IT infrastructure, Icelandic outlet DV reported Friday, citing a joint statement with CERT-IS, Iceland’s national cybersecurity incident response team.

Early findings point to a well-organized and technically sophisticated attack, and the company said it couldn’t rule out that more systems were affected than it had confirmed. It temporarily shut down all services. The statement didn’t mention Wagyu or whether customer data was taken.

Turns out that the entire hosting provider of https://t.co/skXiWey4Fb got hacked with everyone's data + internal server files today.

The XMR1 deployer key was held on an OLD server on there that I DELETED, but…

— PerpetualCow.hl (@PerpetualCow) November 20, 2026

PerpetualCow said he would personally refund everyone who lost money and that Wagyu would relaunch later. “Give me some time please,” he wrote, but didn’t give a timeline.

Bitquery said onchain data alone can’t show whether the deployer’s key was stolen, and that it can’t see how much Monero backs the remaining XMR1, since the Monero side of the bridge isn’t visible.

The attacker’s account received its first funds from the crypto mixer Tornado Cash minutes before it was set up. The same deployer account still controls nearly all of ZEC1, a Zcash token. Wagyu also disclosed a separate data leak in October, saying at the time that funds were unaffected.

Wagyu surfaced earlier this month in ZachXBT’s tracing of a suspected $106 million theft from an unnamed European entity, in which part of the funds were converted to Monero through the service.

The holdings represent 4.98% of Ethereum's total supply of 122.2 million tokens and were worth approximately $10.4 billion as of Nov. 22, according to the Norwalk, Connecticut-based company.

Bitmine said it has staked 5,227,037 ETH worth $9 billion. The company projects $245 million in annualized staking revenue at current levels and up to $286 million once fully staked at its 2.74% seven-day yield.

The company reported combined crypto, cash, marketable securities and "moonshots" holdings of $11.1 billion, including $384 million in cash and marketable securities, 216 BTC, a $180 million stake in Beast Industries and a $54 million stake in Eightco Holdings.

Bitmine Chairman Tom Lee reiterated the company's conviction in ether, pointing to the asset's issuance metrics to argue that it functions as a store of value alongside Bitcoin.

Lee said ETH supply had grown 1.26% since June 30, 2025, when Bitmine adopted its Ethereum treasury strategy. He compared that figure with 0.82% annualized growth for bitcoin, while Solana (SOL), XRP and Cardano (ADA) recorded annualized rates of 7.6%, 5.4% and 2.9%.

"In other words, ETH and BTC have essentially the same inflation rate and both are 'hard money' measured in supply growth," Lee said.

ETH traded at $1,717.20 on Monday, up 0.89% over 24 hours but down 27.97% over the past month.

Bitmine shares gained 8.94% in pre-market trading after closing at $12.98 on Friday. The stock is down 44.43% over the past month and 53.64% year to date.

#4949. Google Gives Gemini Control of Mac Apps, But Its New AI Agent Comes With Risks

Google Gives Gemini Control of Mac Apps, But Its New AI Agent Comes With Risks

Google wants Gemini to do more than answer questions on your Mac.

The company launched a computer-use feature Thursday that lets its AI assistant open applications, navigate menus, fill out forms, and move information between local files and websites on a user's behalf.

“Starting today, computer use in the Gemini app for Mac helps you get multi-step tasks done across your local files, desktop apps, and the web,” Google announced. “Just describe what you need, and Gemini handles the clicking, typing, menus, and forms for you.”

The new capability runs in what Google calls an isolated background window, leaving the computer's main display and cursor available for the owner. Users can watch the agent's actions through a live preview inside the Gemini chat and interrupt a task if it goes off course.

Google's examples include extracting shipping details from PDF documents and entering them into online forms, editing batches of photos, and building presentations from dashboard data. The company also suggests researching prices and preparing resale listings based on pictures stored on a Mac.

Those jobs involve a different kind of AI interaction than asking a chatbot to write instructions. Gemini is supposed to operate the software itself, carrying out the clicks and keystrokes that would otherwise fall to the user.

The feature is rolling out in English in the United States to adult users with personal paid Google accounts. It requires an Apple Silicon Mac running macOS Sequoia 15.0 or later; work and school accounts are not supported at launch.

To activate it, users enable computer use in the Mac app, open Task mode, and prompt Gemini with @Computer use. According to Google's support documentation, the assistant asks for permission before controlling a new app, with options to deny access, allow it once, or allow it on an ongoing basis.

Users can create lists of allowed and blocked apps, monitor the virtual screen, and stop a task. Google says sensitive actions require confirmation before the agent proceeds, though an app marked "always allow" can be accessed again without another app-level prompt.

Computer use adds a local dimension to Google's agent strategy. Decrypt previously covered Gemini Spark, the company's cloud-based task agent, while competitors have developed desktop-control tools including OpenAI's Codex, Anthropic's Claude, and Kimi Work.

The separate window is also a practical distinction: an agent that commandeers the visible cursor can make a computer difficult to use during a long task. Google's approach lets the person keep working while Gemini handles another workflow.

A background window should not be confused with a guarantee that files or accounts are isolated from risk. Google's own documentation warns that the agent can reach active sessions in desktop applications, encounter authentication codes displayed on screen, and take actions that some apps cannot reverse.

In that case, any error is the user’s fault, not Google’s.

It also warns about prompt injection, an attack in which malicious instructions hidden in a document or website persuade an AI system to do something its user never requested. With permission to operate multiple apps, Gemini could encounter hostile content in one program and act on it in another.

Google offers optional on-demand file backups but says they cannot always be created. Where they work, the temporary copies remain available for 24 hours or until the next prompt, and some applications may prevent their content from being backed up at all.

The launch also comes three days after Apple introduced tighter access controls in the macOS 27.3 beta. Those changes give users more ways to restrict powerful permissions to specific apps or folders and to require fresh approval each time access is requested.

#4947. Bitcoin Price Fades Near $61,000 as Stocks Soar on Huang’s Return

The bitcoin price slipped back to near $61,100 on Monday afternoon, giving up nearly all of its morning spike even as Wall Street held one of its biggest rallies in years on Nvidia’s decision to reinstate Jensen Huang as CEO.

Bitcoin jumped above $64,000 shortly after the Nvidia news broke before 10 a.m. ET and topped out near $64,500. The move reversed within hours, and the bitcoin price dipped to around $60,800 by 2:45 p.m. ET. It remains up only modestly on the day.

The round trip was costly for leveraged traders. Bitcoin liquidations reached about $321 million, with short bets making up $224 million of that total. Liquidations across the broader crypto market topped $762 million.

Stocks kept climbing while bitcoin slid. The Nasdaq Composite was up about 6.9% near 24,170 at about 3:40 p.m. ET, while the S&P 500 gained about 5% and the Dow rose about 3.2%. The S&P 500 had fallen seven straight days before Monday.

Nvidia (NVDA) led the move, trading near $165.66, up about 32% from Friday’s close of $125.53. The board voted unanimously to bring Huang back as president, CEO and director, 12 days after he resigned.

A stronger dollar may be weighing on the bitcoin price. The dollar index rose about 0.65% to 104.40, its strongest level since April 2025, as the stock rebound put more Federal Reserve rate hikes back in play. The Fed meets Dec. 8-9, and a quarter-point hike remains the base case.

Adam Haeems, head of asset management at Tesseract Group, pointed to steady selling during U.S. market hours since Huang first stepped down. “Bitcoin has fallen in seven of the eight U.S. sessions since Huang went, today’s included, and last week each session’s fall lined up with that day’s ETF outflow,” he said.

Crypto stocks held up far better than the bitcoin price. Strategy (MSTR) was up about 9.3% near $85.03 in afternoon trading, after it disclosed a purchase of 457 bitcoin last week. Coinbase (COIN) gained about 6.7% to near $108.78, Robinhood (HOOD) rose about 8.8% to near $67.64 and Circle (CRCL) added about 8% to near $51.33.

Miners with AI data center businesses posted the biggest moves. IREN (IREN) jumped about 23% to near $14.57, CleanSpark (CLSK) rose about 12.6% and MARA Holdings (MARA) gained about 10%. Core Scientific (CORZ) climbed about 9.4% and Riot Platforms (RIOT) added about 7.1%.

Monday’s spot bitcoin ETF flows are the next test. The funds lost about $1.79 billion last week, their fourth straight week of net outflows, as bitcoin slid toward $58,000 on Friday and hit a weekend low near $55,100.

“Tonight’s ETF flow figure is the first real test of this bounce,” Haeems said. Strong inflows would suggest buyers are returning, while another round of outflows would raise doubts about how far the rebound can run.

Traders also face Wednesday’s inflation data and thin trading around Thanksgiving in a holiday-shortened week. Wintermute OTC trader Jasper De Maere said he isn’t ready to call a bottom for the bitcoin price. “A few days of data do not confirm a trend and these series turn slowly,” he said.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $61,100.

#4943. Sky cofounder weighs SKY sale to repay $77 million as stUSDS pool runs dry

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Upbit, Bithumb and Coinone flagged SKY and USDS and halted deposits, with Coinone warning of losses for stUSDS holders.

Sky Protocol cofounder Rune Christensen said on Monday that he could potentially sell a portion of his SKY tokens to institutions to repay roughly 77 million USDS in outstanding loans.

This comes as Christensen tweeted Monday that his borrowing led to a withdrawal crunch in Sky's stUSDS lending pool.

Christensen said he had so far repaid around 3 million USDS. Sky's dashboard currently shows $0 available for withdrawals, with 99.99% of the roughly $159 million pool in use and an annualized depositor rate spiking to over 50% from around 6% earlier in the month.

"This is on me," Christensen wrote, saying he had grown overconfident and borrowed without properly considering "what happens if rates spike."

I've just repaid 3M USDS from my Staked SKY borrow positions. I still owe about 77M. To repay the rest, I'm exploring options to sell part of my SKY directly to institutional investors who share Sky's vision of becoming infrastructure that institutions can rely on. That's the same path that made Sky the only stablecoin protocol rated by both S&P and Moody's. I will aim to post continuous updates as the payback happens. This is on me. I got overconfident in Sky's strength borrowed without properly thinking through what happens if rates spike. it was a big mistake, and it's part of why stUSDS can't withdraw today. I'm sorry. What happens next for stUSDS holders: * I am aware of a proposal being made by the governance participants of Sky that will address the stUSDS situation more broadly. * Until then I will be posting updates at least every 48 hours, even when there's nothing new. USDS is holding its peg. stUSDS holders deserves the best possible outcome this situation, and getting them there is my top priority.

Formerly MakerDAO, Sky lets users deposit its USDS stablecoins into the pool in return for stUSDS. The deposits fund loans to borrowers who put up staked SKY as collateral. But with all the USDS lent out, depositors need to wait for borrowers to either repay loans or make new deposits before they can redeem through the pool.

USDS was still trading near $1 at the time of publication.

An ecosystem proposal posted to Sky's governance forum on Monday called for cutting stUSDS rates to 10%, pausing new borrowing and using the protocol's reserves to buy stUSDS on the open market.

Separately, South Korean exchanges Upbit, Bithumb and Coinone put SKY and USDS on trading warning lists and suspended deposits for both. Coinone cited the risk of losses for stUSDS holders and said withdrawal restrictions were ongoing.
