#4891. Google's Quantum Chip Beats Classical Limits in 'Impossible' Game

Google's Quantum Chip Beats Classical Limits in 'Impossible' Game

Google says its Willow quantum processor won a mathematical coordination game 98.3% of the time, above the 88.9% maximum attainable by classical strategies, in a Science study published Thursday.

The experiment tests quantum contextuality, so measurement outcomes depend on what else is measured alongside them. Google researchers explained that “you cannot assign fixed, pre-existing values to everything you might measure.”

Don’t panic. The result does not mean Google has a commercial quantum computer, nor that its largest experiment beat every classical machine. The 98.3% figure came from four qubits—the basic unit of information used to encode data in quantum computing—the 105-qubit run tested a different, more limited challenge.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Google with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

The first test used the Mermin-Peres magic square game, which asks two players to fill intersecting parts of a three-by-three grid without communicating. Their answers must obey conflicting row-and-column rules and match at the shared square.

A prearranged classical strategy can succeed on no more than eight of nine assignments, or 88.9%. Entangled qubits can theoretically win every round, while Google’s physical processor won 98.3% across 900,000 trials, according to the researchers.

The researchers wrote that “our qubits coordinated better than any pre-written table allows.” Unavoidable hardware noise prevented the result from reaching the quantum ideal.

Google also measured contextuality. Any model with predetermined outcomes scores at most 4; quantum theory allows 6. Willow registered 5.590, with a reported uncertainty of 0.008.

A separate GHZ parity game asked up to 71 entangled qubits to deliver coordinated binary answers. Success was measured against the strongest classical winning rate, which approaches 50% with more players; Google remained above that limit throughout its tests, despite accumulating hardware noise.

Researchers also verified genuine entanglement across 45 qubits. In the hidden linear function task, a shallow quantum circuit had to produce a binary string satisfying mathematical constraints. The processor reached roughly 60% success using 105 qubits.

Google says the tasks exposed small, systematic errors that conventional randomized tests can hide. Its corporate blog called contextuality testing “a sensitive new method to catch subtle hardware errors,” potentially helping engineers evaluate circuits used for chemistry and materials research.

The work follows Google's October report of a 40-fold error reduction in preparing magic states, a resource needed for fault-tolerant quantum computing.

For Bitcoin, the looming danger is that a future fault-tolerant quantum computer could break digital signatures, but that is still far from happening. Google’s 105-qubit task was instead a shallow mathematical circuit, and its largest instance still failed in about four out of 10 runs.

Bitcoin Rebounds to $61K After Weekend Plunge to Two-Year Low

Bitcoin recovered to around $61,300 early on Monday after a turbulent weekend that saw it fall as low as $55,298, its lowest level since September 2024, as a slide in tech stocks and expectations of tighter Federal Reserve policy rattled risk assets.

The leading cryptocurrency was up 0.5% over 24 hours at time of publication, but down 4.5% over the past week, according to CoinGecko data. It remains about 51% below its all-time high of $126,080.

The weekend low came a day after Bitcoin broke below $60,000 on Friday, as AI stocks slumped following Anthropic's IPO.

"Bitcoin's sharp volatility over the weekend was primarily the result of a steep pullback in U.S. tech stocks, expectations of further monetary policy tightening by the Federal Reserve, and deleveraging across global risk assets," Tim Sun, senior researcher at HashKey Group, told Decrypt.

If growth expectations keep falling while inflation keeps the Fed hiking, "two key pillars that previously supported risk-asset rallies—ample liquidity and strong growth expectations—could be weakened simultaneously," Sun said. More than $2 billion in leveraged liquidations over the weekend had released some short-term selling pressure, he added, creating conditions for a possible rebound.

Spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $669.2 million in net outflows on Friday, their largest single-day outflow since June 25, according to SoSoValue data. That brought last week's net outflows to $1.79 billion, the funds' third consecutive week of withdrawals topping $1 billion, and November's total so far to $4.14 billion, by Decrypt's calculation. Total net assets fell to $71.3 billion, the lowest since June 30.

"The weekend correction looks less like a Bitcoin-specific event and more like part of a wider market correction," Neil Steinhardt, head of Nexo U.S., told Decrypt, noting that gold and equities also fell. ETF outflows had worsened the drawdown, he said, adding that equities appeared to have led the sell-off.

It is now below its median since launch, while outflows run near $260M per day.

The last stretch this quiet, in mid-2024, came before the run to $100K. Periods of apathy tend to be better times to accumulate than periods of hype. pic.twitter.com/xiJ3HF9cLj

— glassnode (@glassnode) November 22, 2026

Onchain analytics firm Glassnode took a longer view. ETF trading volume has fallen below its median since the funds launched, while outflows are running near $260 million a day, the firm tweeted on Sunday. The last stretch this quiet, in mid-2024, preceded Bitcoin's run to $100,000, it said.

BitMEX co-founder Arthur Hayes struck a more bearish note. Bitcoin had "decisively" broken below its previous local low, he tweeted on Saturday, adding that "$50,000 will be the next real test" if the Nasdaq kept falling. On Sunday evening, as Nasdaq futures weakened in Asian trading, Hayes tweeted that Bitcoin could retest $55,000, and noted that South Korea's Kospi index had fallen 5%.

$BTC just decisively broke below the previous local low. If Nasdaq keeps puking, $50,000 will be the next real test. pic.twitter.com/vn0VSpuWAz

— Arthur Hayes (@CryptoHayes) November 21, 2026

On Decrypt Predict, users put a 17% chance on Bitcoin falling to $55,000 before the end of November, as of 5:56 a.m. ET on Monday. The market settles on Binance's BTC/USDT price, which fell as low as $55,130 last week, according to the Binance chart Hayes shared. Users put a 1% chance on Bitcoin climbing back to $82,500 this month.

Traders now turn to October's personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation data, due on Wednesday, followed by the November jobs report on December 4 and the Fed's December 9 meeting.

The core PCE report "could help show whether the Fed is leaning toward 25 or 50 basis points," Steinhardt said, with markets then guided by the December meeting and comments from Fed Chair Kevin Warsh and other officials.

Traders are leaning toward the smaller move. As of 6:00 a.m. ET on Monday, CME FedWatch put the odds of a quarter-point hike on December 9 at 96.2%, up from 88.6% on Friday, with a 3.8% chance that rates stay unchanged. The odds of a half-point increase stood at zero, from 4.5% a week earlier.

U.S. spot selling remained heavy last week, according to a note from Bitfire Research shared with Decrypt. The Coinbase Bitcoin Premium Index, which tracks the gap between Bitcoin's price on Coinbase and on Binance, fell as low as negative 0.23%, the firm said.

Sun said he saw "no clear bullish catalyst" over the next one to two weeks, and expected Bitcoin to stay in a "high-volatility consolidation phase." Cooler inflation, steadier stocks and a return of ETF inflows could support a more sustainable recovery, he said, while hotter inflation and further tech losses could send Bitcoin back to its prior lows.

#4885. Bitcoin ETFs shed $1.8B in third-largest weekly outflow

Bitcoin ETF outflows reached $4.14 billion in November through Friday, nearing June’s $4.51 billion total.

US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) recorded $1.79 billion in net outflows last week, their third-largest weekly total since the funds began trading in January 2024.

The record was $2.61 billion in the week ended Feb. 28, 2025, according to SoSoValue data. The week ended June 26 ranked second, with about $2 million more in net outflows than last week, based on the reported daily figures.

Investors withdrew a net $4.34 billion over four consecutive weeks, bringing year-to-date net outflows to roughly $3.8 billion.

BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT), the largest US spot Bitcoin ETF by assets, accounted for $1.37 billion, or about 76% of last week’s net outflows.

On Thursday, IBIT recorded $541.1 million in outflows, surpassing its previous daily high of $528.3 million on Jan. 30.

The fund broke that record again on Friday, with $544.7 million in net outflows, bringing the two-day total to $1.09 billion.

Monthly spot Bitcoin ETF flows. Source: SoSoValue

November net outflows across US spot Bitcoin ETFs reached $4.14 billion through Friday, about 8% below June’s record of $4.51 billion.

Bitcoin fell to just above $55,000 on Saturday, before recovering above $60,000 later that day.

Crypto trader Merlijn The Trader wrote on Sunday that Bitcoin (BTC) had fallen below the lowest band on the Rainbow Chart, a color-coded model that tracks Bitcoin’s long-term price trend.

#4883. Solana Meme Coin for Fiona the Hippo Raises $100K for Cincinnati Zoo Charity

Solana Meme Coin for Fiona the Hippo Raises $100K for Cincinnati Zoo Charity

Fiona the Hippo, a fan-favorite pygmy hippo at the Cincinnati Zoo, died last week after swallowing a hat that accidentally ended up in her enclosure. Crypto bros have created a meme coin in her honor—raising $100,000 for charity in the process.

The tragic death came after a visitor tossed their knitted hat in the air, which then got caught by a ceiling fan and flung it through a small window into Fiona's habitat. The pygmy hippo lost her appetite as the hat got stuck in her small intestine, and veterinary staff tried their best to help Fiona pass the hat naturally—as surgery was deemed too risky. Unfortunately, Fiona died on Thursday, November 19, at nine years old.

As is often the case, crypto bros rushed to create a Solana meme coin that surged to a $3.52 million market capitalization the day after Fiona's death. It has since dropped more than 50% to $1.5 million, per DEX Screener.

Fiona the hippo has died after she ate a visitor's hat that fell into her enclosure at the Cincinnati Zoo. pic.twitter.com/YHbjoEEDhb

The Pump.fun-created token is donating 100% of the generated creator rewards to the Zoological Society of Cincinnati, the non-profit that runs the zoo and its scientific programs. So far, according to Donate.gg, $103,600 has been donated.

But, of course, profits are at the forefront of crypto traders' minds. One of the top traders turned a $1,637 investment into $43,800 in a matter of days, according to DEX Screener.

"I feel like animals are often treated more like attractions for people than living beings. Their safety and lives should come first, not just entertaining visitors," pseudonymous trader Athena Alpha told Decrypt. "This isn’t even the first time something like this has happened."

The most famous example happened in the same zoo. Endangered gorilla Harambe was fatally shot at the Cincinnati Zoo a decade ago, after a young boy fell into his habitat. This sparked global outrage, as the gorilla was killed through no fault of his own. In turn, Harambe became one of the most viral memes of the 2010s.

"I think stricter action needs to be taken against both visitors and zoo officials to make sure things like this don’t happen again," the trader said. "Honestly, I feel like a big part of the problem is the lack of accountability and consequences. Unless people are held responsible, nothing is really going to change."

The Cincinnati Zoo needs to ensure something like this can never happen again. https://t.co/vWeb8ekyVw pic.twitter.com/hqt35SOcP6

— Nati Sports (@Nati_Sports) November 19, 2026

This story has hit particularly hard, as Fiona was a beloved animal whose premature birth in 2017 drew attention. At birth, zoo keepers had to care for her because she was too small for her mother to look after. Fortunately, she survived this tough start to life and went on to become one of the zoo's most famous animals.

People's love for Fiona may have been boosted by the rise of Moo Deng, a cute pygmy hippo who went mega viral in 2024. As with Fiona, crypto traders made an unofficial meme coin for Moo Deng, but this one went much higher—peaking at a $610 million market cap.

Naturally, traders have made the comparison between the two pygmy hippos, with some suggesting that the Moo Deng meme coin's success should mean similar gains for the Fiona token.

With Fiona's meme coin sitting at a $1.5 million market cap, that thesis has yet to be proven.

"If Fiona goes to $1 billion, her legacy will live forever all around the world," one trader tweeted. "Do it for Fiona!"

#4880. ‘MoldyRoost’ ads can steal crypto wallet recovery phrases from iPhones

The campaign used at least 480 ad domains to target users in more than 47 countries and could infect a vulnerable iPhone with a single tap.

Hackers are using fake ads to spread malware that can steal crypto wallet recovery phrases from vulnerable iPhones, cybersecurity company Confiant said Friday.

Confiant, which calls the campaign MoldyRoost, linked at least 480 ad domains to it between May 12 and Nov. 17. The ads targeted users in more than 47 countries.

“Just loading the landing page of a MoldyRoost ad on a vulnerable iPhone is enough to get infected,” Confiant said in its report.

Google Threat Intelligence reported in March that hackers were using a tool called Coruna to target visitors to fake crypto sites. MoldyRoost bought ads to spread the same tool on sites unrelated to crypto, where tapping an ad could expose a recovery phrase saved on a vulnerable iPhone.

Coruna can search Apple Notes for wallet seed phrases and read QR codes in saved images. A thief who gets a seed phrase can use those words to open the wallet on another device and move the funds.

The campaign also used DarkSword, another tool that can steal wallet data, which Google researchers also warned of in March.

In a Nov. 6 warning about DarkSword, blockchain security firm CertiK urged iPhone users to update their software and keep seed phrases out of Notes, photos and screenshots. It also advised avoiding unfamiliar links and using a hardware wallet for larger holdings.

#4878. Czech Authorities Charge 15 Over $12M Unlicensed Crypto Casino

Czech Authorities Charge 15 Over $12M Unlicensed Crypto Casino

Czech customs officers and police have charged 15 people over BETFIN, a crypto casino that investigators say operated without a gambling license while presenting itself as a decentralized project, authorities said on Thursday.

The group's unlawful gains so far total about 267 million koruna ($12.1 million), according to a joint statement from the Czech customs service and the National Centre Against Organised Crime (NCOZ). Officers detained 20 people and searched more than 40 homes and other premises in October, in an operation involving nearly 300 customs officers and police, as well as Europol staff.

Three alleged organizers are being held in custody. Those charged are accused of participating in an organized criminal group and operating gambling without a license, and the main organizers face up to 13.5 years in prison if convicted. Authorities seized assets worth about 100 million koruna ($4.5 million), including property, vehicles, jewelry, cash and crypto.

The agencies described it as the first Czech case in which suspected illegal gambling centered on a decentralized online casino using blockchain infrastructure, smart contracts and its own token.

BETFIN presented itself as a decentralized casino with its own token traded on decentralized exchanges. Investigators hired a specialist firm to examine its smart contracts on the Polygon network, and analyzed more than 2.3 million bets placed by about 28,500 players. The project was promoted on social media, Telegram and Discord, as well as in online videos and at conferences.

Users could join in two ways, Michal Šimůnek, director of the customs service's search and supervision section, told a press conference near Prague on Thursday, according to the Czech News Agency (ČTK). They could buy tokens to play casino games such as roulette, or buy them as investors and become co-owners of the casino. Despite its decentralized branding, the group ran a centrally controlled system, he said.

A large share of player accounts were created by automated programs to inflate activity, Šimůnek said. "This created the casino's supposed profit, which was then redistributed to investors," he said, in comments translated from Czech.

Developers issued billions of tokens and steered investors to buy them off-exchange, which let the group raise money without moving the token's market price, Šimůnek added. Customs officers also found that the declared value of all tokens in circulation, more than 45 billion koruna ($2 billion), far exceeded the roughly 11 million koruna ($500,000) of liquidity on exchanges.

Czech news site Seznam Zprávy reported the raids in October, saying those detained included YouTubers who had promoted BETFIN, and that the project's token had already fallen close to zero in 2025.

The token, BET, has fallen further since. It traded at about $0.000001 on Monday, more than 99.9% below its all-time high of $0.002906, and hit a new all-time low within the past week, according to CoinGecko data. Its 24-hour trading volume was about $1,100, with both CoinGecko and CoinMarketCap putting its total supply at around 777.8 billion tokens.

NCOZ deputy director Petr Danko said using blockchain technology is not illegal, but using it outside the law is. The Czech state is the injured party, he said, because the group was required to obtain authorization to run gambling. Whether investors suffered losses will depend on the ongoing investigation, he added.

The High Prosecutor's Office in Olomouc is overseeing the case. Criminal proceedings are continuing, and authorities said they could not share further details.

Nasdaq-100 futures rose 3.1% before Monday’s open, while Wednesday’s US inflation report could shape expectations for a December rate hike.

Bitcoin (BTC) climbed to $61,846 on Monday, up 12.3% from Saturday’s two-year low, as US stock futures rebounded after last week’s tech sell-off.

Nasdaq-100 futures rose 3.1% and S&P 500 futures gained 2.4% ahead of Monday’s US open after Friday’s tech sell-off.

Both the S&P 500 and the Dow Jones Industrial Average ended Friday in correction territory, more than 10% below their recent closing highs. The Nasdaq Composite fell 5.3% to 22,617.73, erasing its gains for the year.

Nasdaq Composite Index weekly chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

KOSPI one-day chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Amid mounting concerns over long-term Fed policy tightening, traders are focused on the Fed’s “preferred” inflation gauge.

The US Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA) will release the October personal consumption expenditures (PCE) price index on Wednesday. Economists expect prices to have risen 0.3% in October, down from 0.5% in September. They expect annual inflation to rise to 3.8% from 3.6%.

US consumer spending changes in September 2026. Source: BEA

Fed officials have since been more vocal about the need to contain inflation, which has remained above its 2% target for five years. Fed Governor Christopher Waller said last week that policymakers should raise rates by half a percentage point in December or make clear they intend to raise rates again in January.

Markets continue to expect a 25-basis-point hike in December, per data from CME Group’s FedWatch Tool, with a surprise reading in PCE still able to shift the odds, as well as spark volatility in crypto and risk assets.

“Many people believe that they may be making a policy mistake if they overtighten,” Bessent told CBS.

likened conditions to key swing lows from previous BTC price cycles: the crash in Q4 2015, the COVID-19 cross-market crash in March 2020 and the collapse of exchange FTX in November 2022.

#4873. Strategy Buys $30M in Bitcoin, Moves $1B From USD Reserve to Cash Pot

Strategy Buys $30M in Bitcoin, Moves $1B From USD Reserve to Cash Pot

Strategy bought 457 BTC for $29.6 million last week and moved $1 billion out of the reserve that backs its dividend payments into a general-purpose cash account, the company said on Monday.

The Bitcoin treasury firm paid an average of $64,898 per coin between November 16 and November 22, according to an SEC filing. Its fourth straight weekly purchase lifted its holdings to 851,305 BTC, acquired for a total of $64.23 billion at an average of about $75,446 per coin.

Strategy has acquired 457 $BTC and repurchased $212M of $STRC. We increased our Digital Credit Securities Repurchase Program by $1B and reallocated $1B from our USD Reserve to USD Cash. As of 11/22/26, we hold 851,305 BTC and $4.8B of USD Assets. $MSTR https://t.co/Wklom6dzrm

— Michael Saylor (@saylor) November 23, 2026

At the current Bitcoin price of about $61,233, per Decrypt data, those holdings are worth about $52.1 billion, roughly $12.1 billion less than Strategy paid. Bitcoin fell as low as $55,298 over the weekend, its lowest level since September 2024.

Executive chairman Michael Saylor tweeted that, as of November 22, Strategy held 851,305 BTC and $4.8 billion in U.S. dollar assets.

Strategy's board authorized management to move up to $1 billion from its USD Reserve, which is meant to cover dividends on its preferred stock and interest on its debt, to its USD Cash account. Management can use that account for broader purposes, including buying Bitcoin, topping up the reserve and other capital management.

After the move, the USD Reserve stood at $3.73 billion and USD Cash at $1.09 billion. The reserve held $4.77 billion on November 8, according to an earlier filing. Strategy drew $17.6 million from it to pay preferred dividends last week.

Strategy repurchased 2,183,747 shares of STRC, its variable-rate preferred stock, for $211.8 million, down from $228 million the week before. It funded the Bitcoin purchase and $147 million of the buyback by selling 1,925,676 shares of MSTR stock for $176.7 million in net proceeds, and paid the remaining $64.8 million from USD Cash.

The board also raised the cap on its digital credit securities repurchase program, which covers its preferred stock, from $3 billion to $4 billion, leaving $1.12 billion available. Before the increase, about $120 million remained, after two weeks of STRC buybacks totaling about $440 million. Another $1 billion remains available to buy back MSTR stock.

The changes come a week before index provider MSCI removes MSTR from its MSCI USA Index after trading closes on November 30, which also takes it out of broader benchmarks such as the MSCI World index. Strategy has estimated that funds linked to MSCI indexes hold about 13 million of its shares.

#4870. ECB to let banks pledge more tokenized securities as collateral

The ECB aims to start accepting securities issued on platforms licensed under the EU’s distributed ledger pilot program in June 2027.

The European Central Bank (ECB) approved a plan Monday to let banks borrow from euro area central banks against tokenized securities issued on platforms licensed under an EU pilot program.

The ECB plans to put the change into effect in June 2027, according to its announcement. The EU’s DLT Pilot Regime allows licensed platforms to handle trading or settlement of securities using distributed ledger technology.

Since March 30, banks have been able to pledge qualifying tokenized securities issued through central securities depositories that use distributed ledgers. Under Monday’s decision, a bank holding a qualifying bond issued on a licensed pilot platform could also use it to secure a central bank loan.

The securities must still meet the existing collateral rules of the Eurosystem, which comprises the ECB and the national central banks of the euro area. They must also be available to the Eurosystem through an approved link to a central securities depository that offers settlement on TARGET2-Securities, the Eurosystem’s securities settlement platform.

The Eurosystem expects to publish additional legal and operational requirements for the pilot platforms in spring 2027.

“A future-proof collateral framework must embrace new technologies without compromising on safety or the rigour of our risk assessment,” said ECB executive board member Isabel Schnabel.

The ECB said it is also exploring how banks could pledge securities issued and settled entirely on distributed ledgers. Through its Appia project, the Eurosystem plans to publish a blueprint in 2028 for how Europe’s tokenized wholesale financial markets could operate.

Related: EU taps industry to set standards for tokenized securities

#4867. Kashmir’s Textile Legacy Inspires a Sumptuous New Line of Home and Fashion Accessories

Few artisanal traditions are so linked with a place that its geographical namesake winds up in the global lexicon. In the case of fine wool shawls from Kashmir—prized among 19th-century Europeans, including Joséphine Bonaparte—the word cashmere stuck. For Kadri Mujtaba, a fascination with these textiles came early. Born into a family of merchants and weavers in the region’s city of Srinagar, he observed a deep-rooted culture around hand-spun yarn and small wooden looms.

A bouquet of lotus pods held up against the brand’s Mountain blanket in pine

A Doorway Sozni twill blanket draped over a chair

“It’s the original work-from-home idea,” he says of the technique, passed down for centuries. Amid encroaching mechanization, Mujtaba has stayed committed to slow craft, cultivating a network of some 2,000 artisans over the past two decades. Initially he’d create custom pieces for designers and brands (Pierre Yovanovitch, The Row, Luis Laplace) and host high-end trunk shows. That is how Jonathan Renker—via his father, a longtime customer—first encountered the wares. Now, he and Mujtaba have partnered on Kadri: a new brand of cashmere accessories and home goods that marry Kashmiri refinement and streamlined design.

A Jaali Kaani blanket on the grounds of Pari Mahal garden

A wood swift of cashmere yarn at the Kadri atelier

Shawls and scarves from Kadri’s debut collection

The company debuts with a collection of scarves, throws, and blankets. The cofounders enlisted creative director Dan Thawley and textile designer Maeva Elfassi, who distilled the Kashmir Valley landscape into shades of tobacco, lime, vermilion, and—as a nod to Srinagar’s Dal Lake—dusty blue. Wool from Changthangi goats, which graze across the plateaus of nearby Ladakh, is spun by hand, resulting in airy, ultrasoft knits. More impressive still is the subtlety on display. A bouclé piece has the immersive pull of a Color Field painting, while a quilted scarf joins two paper-thin layers. Just one blanket requires months of work and a balletic dance of bobbins.

“What makes Kadri so special is this research-and-development approach,” says Renker, describing a push toward novel applications while holding fast to the handmade vernacular. “The only electricity that’s used are the overhead lights on a cloudy day.”

#4866. Critics of Netflix’s FTX drama point to Obama’s producer credit

Series creator Graham Moore said he invented a scene in which Changpeng Zhao urges Alameda Research to trade ahead of FTX customers’ orders.

Critics who say Netflix’s new FTX drama favors Sam Bankman-Fried over Binance co-founder Changpeng “CZ” Zhao have pointed to former US President Barack Obama’s role as an executive producer.

DRW founder Don Wilson said Sunday that Netflix’s FTX drama The Altruists portrayed Bankman-Fried too sympathetically at the expense of Zhao.

In the fourth episode, Zhao’s character urges Bankman-Fried and Caroline Ellison to give Alameda Research advance access to FTX customers’ orders so it can trade ahead of them.

Series creator Graham Moore identified the scene as fiction in a Friday essay for Vulture. Moore wrote that he believed the pressure the pair faced was real, though “there’s no evidence that CZ ever specifically pressured FTX to front-run.”

Zhao called a clip from the series “complete BS” on Friday, a day after the eight-episode drama premiered. Investment research firm Farside Investors also singled out the front-running scene, saying the show cast Zhao as the villain.

Wilson called the clip an “epic fail by Netflix.” In a second post, he cited the former US president’s executive producer credit. Netflix lists both Barack and Michelle Obama as executive producers and names Moore and Jacqueline Hoyt as showrunners.

In a Sunday post on X, an account called StarPlatinum pointed to the Obamas’ producer credits and Bankman-Fried’s donations to Democrats. The post did not offer evidence that the donations influenced the show.

Related: Sam Bankman-Fried wrote to actor portraying him in Netflix series

Bankman-Fried made about $40 million in publicly reported political donations during the 2022 election cycle, mostly to Democratic candidates and groups. Federal prosecutors later said he also used FTX customer funds to make political contributions to candidates from both parties.

In November 2022, Zhao announced that Binance would sell its holdings of FTT, FTX’s token.

At Bankman-Fried’s 2023 trial, Ellison, Alameda’s former CEO, said she thought Zhao’s announcement “contributed to the situation.” She said FTX collapsed primarily because Alameda could not repay more than $10 billion in FTX customer funds.

Bankman-Fried was sentenced to 25 years in prison in 2024 for orchestrating a fraud that took more than $8 billion from FTX customers.

FTX’s bankruptcy estate sued Binance and Zhao in November 2024, seeking $1.76 billion paid in a 2021 share repurchase. The estate also sought damages over Zhao’s public statements. Binance called the claims “meritless.”

In July, US Bankruptcy Judge Karen Owens dismissed the claims over Zhao’s statements because FTX’s own misconduct barred them. She allowed the estate to pursue its separate claim over the buyback.

#4864. Morning Minute: Crypto Rebounds after NVDA Drives Friday Selloff

Morning Minute: Crypto Rebounds after NVDA Drives Friday Selloff

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

Nvidia closed Friday at $125.53, down 47.5% from its October high, which works out to roughly $2.7 trillion of market value gone in seven weeks. The company beat earnings last week and guided revenue higher, and the stock still fell 23% across the five sessions.

The Nasdaq dropped 10% on the week, its worst since 2020, erasing its entire 2026 gain and shedding about $8.7 trillion since October 6. The S&P and Dow both closed in correction, AMD lost 20.5%, and CoreWeave now trades below its IPO price. Bank of America’s fund manager survey showed investors cutting tech exposure by the most since 2006, with 52% expecting at least one major hyperscaler to announce a capex cut inside six months.

Polymarket traders put 54% odds that the AI bubble will burst in 2026.

Crypto rode the same elevator down. Bitcoin hit roughly $55,100 Friday night, its lowest level since 2024 and marking a new cycle low, 56% off last October’s peak. Fear and Greed printed 9 and ETFs bled $1.79 billion on the week. It’s now tracking toward two consecutive down years, which has never happened before.

Then came the weekend bounce. Bitcoin reclaimed $60,000 and sits over $61,000 this morning; ETH is back above $1,710, and even Anthropic, down 9% Friday, quotes near $90 on Hyperliquid’s perp, still above its $83 offer.

Two explanations landed over the weekend.

U.S. President Donald Trump posted Sunday that the market is down $6 trillion since the Democrats’ “rather strange” win on November 3, that the Nasdaq is down 20%, and that it’s “100% due to” Democratic policy. Looks like no Trump bailout is coming anytime soon.

Elon Musk’s version is more useful. He warned Saturday that the cash shortage next year is “going to be nuts,” because even a few months of lag between paying for compute and earning revenue off it means hundreds of billions in incremental working capital. Then the question: where will that come from?

That’s a funding problem rather than a valuation one, and it lands harder coming from him. SpaceX reportedly sold $32 billion of bonds last week to buy Nvidia chips, so he’s describing a squeeze he’s already borrowing into.

So where does this leave us? The Fed meets December 8th and 9th with the market pricing a hike into all of this. Gas prices just hit an eight-month low ahead of Thanksgiving, down about 15% in a month, which should take some pressure off the headline print. The catch is that pump prices are still running 18% above last year, and core CPI, the number Waller actually cited when he floated fifty basis points, strips energy out entirely. Any relief has to come from shelter and services cooling instead.

Perhaps the Fed provides some. Whether it’s enough to stop the bleeding, only time will tell.

#4862. AI agents are using credit cards. Where does that leave crypto?

AI agents are already using credit cards to transact, raising questions about where crypto fits in the emerging agent economy.

The idea that crypto will become the default payment choice for autonomous AI agents is widespread in the industry. The thesis is that because AI agents can’t open bank accounts and need to make lots of transactions, they will inexorably gravitate to instant, decentralized and low cost crypto payments.But the credit card industry has no intention of letting that happen.

The Wall Street Journal reported on Nov. 19 that AI agents are already using credit cards to make purchases on consumers’ behalf, and banks and payment companies are racing to accommodate them. Crypto trader and co-host of the 1000x podcast Jonah Van Bourg posted a screenshot, and commented:

But experts argue the jury is still out. Professor emeritus of finance at Marquette University, David Krause, tells Magazine that credit cards offer some advantages, but high costs could be their downfall in certain scenarios.

Stablecoins, he says, make much more sense for “those situations involving micropayments.”

Credit card payments are the default for most human-initiated transactions on the web. So an agent tasked with booking a flight or a hotel room may also default to this type of payment as well.

Related: DeepSeek developing AI agent requiring less human input: Report

“When an agent books your flight or reorders your groceries, it’s acting for a person, at a merchant that already accepts cards, with consumer protections people expect, like chargebacks,” Lewis Nurcombe, chief revenue officer at WalletConnect, tells Magazine.

Visa’s recent paper on the rise of agentic commerce says tokenization can give agents permission to transact without needing to expose the cardholder’s underlying payment details, and spending controls can restrict the purchases they can make.

So when the merchant already accepts cards and has familiar mechanisms for handling disputed transactions, it’s hard to argue the virtues of introducing an entirely new payment system.

But the economics start to change when agents begin paying other machines.

AI agents may create a new economy of machine-to-machine transactions, where agents buy access to a dataset, request computing power, or pay to access a single piece of information. Many of these transactions may only be worth a fraction of a cent and potentially need to occur thousands of times without a human approving each one.

“If an agent wants to pay a few cents for one API call or one piece of data, card fees don’t make sense,” Aitor Argomaniz, chief executive of AI payments company Nevermined, tells Magazine.

That’s because card networks were designed around transactions big enough to absorb fixed processing fees, rather than micro-payments.

Stablecoins offer a different model. Agents can hold funds in a wallet, make payments programmatically and settle transactions onchain without going through a conventional checkout process.

Nurcombe says that stablecoins are most useful “wherever the transaction is too small, too frequent or too global for cards to make sense.”

x402 is an open, neutral standard for internet-native payments. Source: x402

Nevermined compared 100 API calls priced at 10 cents each. Using a conventional card via Stripe would not be an option as the payments infrastructure network rejects charges under $0.50. But using stablecoins, the company estimates the same 100 payments would cost around 10 cents in total using Coinbase’s x402 facilitator on Base.

That doesn’t mean crypto payments will automatically win however, as a number of microtransactions could be aggregated into a single card payment. Stablecoin transactions also carry costs related to infrastructure, conversion, custody and liquidity.

The Bank for International Settlements (BIS) found that stablecoin transfers can still incur trading, conversion and withdrawal fees, while limited liquidity between stablecoins and local currencies can add further friction.

In the transition to agentic finance, there’s a more fundamental question to overcome: who is responsible when an agent gets something wrong?

Related: Banks are banning AI agents over security... and competition concerns

“I tell my students the real question isn’t ‘crypto or cards,’ it’s who solves the agent identity and liability attribution issues,” Krause says.

One partial solution is a separate wallet funded with a limited amount of money that could prevent an agent from accidentally draining a user’s main account.

WalletConnect is developing ways for users to give agents access to dedicated wallets while retaining control over the rest of their funds.

“The most an agent can ever spend is what the person chose to give it,” Nurcombe says.

But spending limits only mitigate the damage. They can’t determine who is liable when an agent makes an unauthorized purchase, buys the wrong service or falls victim to manipulation. Nor do they provide the kind of resolution consumers expect from established payment providers.

Nevermined compared 100 API calls priced at 10 cents each. Source: Nevermined.

That may mean banks and card networks have an advantage beyond just having more merchants on their systems; they already have relationships with customers, businesses and regulators, as well as established processes for handling disputes.

Krause says that stacks the odds in traditional finance’s favor. “My bet is the large TradFi players will ultimately win,” he says. “They’re the ones with funding access and are positioned to straddle the divide and offer a hybrid version.”

Nevermined supports payments using both cards and stablecoins, and Argomaniz expects the two systems to coexist, “at least for the next few years.”

This is where Van Bourg’s argument becomes harder for the crypto industry to dismiss — even if stablecoins are useful for machine-to-machine payments, established payment networks could just incorporate them without handing over control of the customer relationship.

So an agent might interact with Visa or Mastercard while the networks use blockchain infrastructure behind the scenes.

That would effectively turn blockchain into payment plumbing without businesses or consumers needing to hold crypto or interact with a decentralized protocol.

Mastercard completed its acquisition of stablecoin infrastructure provider BVNK in August, in a deal announced at up to $1.8 billion, giving Mastercard the infrastructure to connect fiat and onchain payments.

So, while AI agents could use blockchain-based payments where existing rails struggle, crypto could find itself powering the agent economy without capturing much of its value.

Magazine: How to preserve wealth and access funds during a national emergency

#4860. Nvidia Reinstates Jensen Huang as CEO Days After Board Ruled Out His Return

Nvidia Reinstates Jensen Huang as CEO Days After Board Ruled Out His Return

Nvidia's board has reinstated Jensen Huang as president and CEO, six days after its chair said directors did not intend to bring him back.

The board voted unanimously to reinstate Huang as president, CEO and a board member, effective immediately, the company said on Monday. It followed "a series of discussions and meetings" with Huang, after which the board was satisfied with his "candor and ability to lead" the firm.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Nvidia with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

In reaching its decision, the board said it received input from "senior levels of the United States government," and based on that input, it is confident that Huang's reinstatement "poses no national security risk." The statement did not name the officials involved.

"The Board believes Jensen is the right person to lead NVIDIA, and that his reinstatement is in the best interests of our stockholders, employees, partners and customers," board chair Harvey Jones said. Jones will remain chair, and Colette Kress, who served as interim CEO, continues as chief financial officer.

NVIDIA Board reinstates Jensen Huang as President, CEO and Board Director.

Read the release: https://t.co/x4bnjiSVil

— NVIDIA Newsroom (@nvidianewsroom) November 23, 2026

"I take this responsibility with the greatest humility and renewed commitment," Huang said in the statement. "I can't wait to get back to work."

The decision overturns the board's position from last week. "The Nvidia board does not intend to reinstate Jensen as CEO," Jones said on the company's November 17 earnings call, as Nvidia hired executive search firm Heidrick & Struggles to find a permanent replacement.

Hours earlier, Huang had offered to return if the board decided it was in the best interest of shareholders, employees, the company "and America." His comeback had drawn support from President Donald Trump and Elon Musk, even as CNBC reported that directors were not considering it.

Huang resigned on November 12 after reports of extramarital relationships, including one with Alice Huang, a former executive at Singapore-based Nvidia customer Megaspeed whom U.S. officials suspected of being a Chinese intelligence asset. China has denied that she is a spy.

The independent directors' committee investigating whether Huang was the target of a foreign intelligence effort "will proceed to conclusion," Nvidia said, and Huang has said he will cooperate with it.

The company also pointed to its new National Security and Export Control Committee, made up of independent directors, and said it is searching for a chief compliance officer to oversee compliance across the company. Nvidia said it will keep cooperating with government investigations and inquiries.

Huang still faces scrutiny in Washington. House committees have asked him to sit for a closed-door interview on December 1, and Senate Banking Committee Chairman Tim Scott (R-SC) has asked him to testify at a public hearing on December 3.

Nvidia shares rose to over $160 in premarket trading on Monday, up about 28% from Friday's close of $125.53, according to Yahoo! Finance. That would add roughly $780 billion to the company's market value, but still leave the stock below its $166.19 close on the day Huang resigned.

#4856. Bitcoin Rallies, Anthropic Sinks as Nvidia Surges 35% on Jensen Huang’s Return

Bitcoin Rallies, Anthropic Sinks as Nvidia Surges 35% on Jensen Huang’s Return

Wall Street welcomed Jensen Huang back to Nvidia with lots of green confetti today by sending the chipmaker’s shares up nearly 35%, helping drag battered technology stocks and crypto prices out of last week’s rout.

Nvidia traded around $169.83 at 10:28 a.m. ET, up 34.97% from Friday’s close. It is the biggest bullish jump in the history of the company.

And the FOMO contagion spread quickly to other tech stocks. Rival AMD jumped 8.91% to $462.09, while the iShares Semiconductor ETF (SOXX) surged 15% to $141.77.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Nvidia with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

Bitcoin joined the party, trading near $63,500 after dipping below $60,000 during Friday’s selloff. Ethereum climbed to about $1,790, and cash CFDs tracking the Nasdaq 100 and S&P 500 rebounded to roughly 26,950 and 7,285, respectively.

The catalyst was Nvidia’s announcement that its founder Jensen Huang had been reinstated as president, CEO and director, effective immediately. The board voted unanimously just six days after publicly ruling out his return—and even doubling down on their decision several times.

Huang resigned November 12 amid reports of extramarital relationships, including one with a businesswoman U.S. officials suspected of ties to Chinese intelligence. Beijing has denied the espionage allegation, and no public evidence has established that Huang knowingly passed company secrets to China.

Colette Kress, Nvidia’s finance chief, took over as interim CEO while the company launched a search for a permanent replacement. On November 17, board chair Harvey Jones said directors did not intend to reinstate Huang, despite a campaign for his return backed by Silicon Valley executives and President Donald Trump.

Monday’s reversal followed meetings with Huang and input from senior U.S. government officials. The board said it was satisfied with his candor and leadership ability and was confident his return posed “no national security risk,” though its independent investigation will continue.

“I can’t wait to get back to work,” Huang said in the statement. Kress will remain chief financial officer, and Jones will stay on as board chair.

“It tells us to some extent that Jensen Huang was too big to be fired,” Interactive Brokers strategist Steve Sosnick told Reuters.

Prediction markets were caught off guard, too. A Polymarket contract on Huang rejoining Nvidia by November 30 traded near 99% after the announcement, compared with roughly 3% shortly beforehand.

His return also tanked the probabilities of the AI bubble bursting. After his outsting, Polymarket odds skyrocketed to more than 70%, minutes after his reinstatement, the odds dropped to 14%.

The rally followed an ugly stretch for AI stocks. Nvidia closed Friday at $125.53, about 47.5% below its October high, while the Nasdaq lost roughly 10% over the previous week. Bitcoin fell toward $55,100 Friday night before staging a weekend recovery.

Monday’s bounce pushed Nvidia above the $166.19 closing price recorded on the day Huang resigned. Bitcoin, meanwhile, recovered the $63,000 area, but its daily chart still shows a bearish moving-average configuration and a relative strength index near 34, indicating that the recent relief rally has not erased the broader downtrend.

Overall, crypto traders are still timid but evidently don’t think this is the end of the world.

On the chart, Bitcoin faces initial resistance around $65,030, followed by $68,090. On the downside, $61,240 and the psychological $60,000 mark are immediate levels to watch; another break lower would put the recent $55,100 area back in focus.

One of the few notable losers was Anthropic, whose stock slipped 3.16% to $85.81. That left the Claude developer about 25% below its post-IPO peak near $115, although still slightly above the $83 offering price set for its $81.34 billion public offering last Thursday. Nvidia itself committed about $10 billion to a separate, conditional private investment in Anthropic alongside the IPO.

The decline looks more like investors continuing to digest a pricey, volatile listing than a reaction to Huang’s reinstatement. But the internet has another theory: memes and posts have cast Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei as playing a role in Huang’s ouster because of his support towards open-weight models and his decision to buy Hugging Face, the world’s largest repository of open source AI models, to protect the ecosystem.

Huang championed broad access to open models, while Amodei has argued for tighter safeguards around powerful AI systems, especially warning against open source AI competing against close models like the ones his company develops, a position that has been widely criticized by influential figures in the AI scene like Yan Lecun who called his mindset “a new form of medieval obscurantism.”

Dario's spiel since GPT-2 has always been about hyping the dangers so that open models would be made illegal and *he* would stay in control.He left OpenAI because he wasn't going to have control.It's like the Ottoman Empire banning the printing press for Arabic so they would…

Nvidia’s independent inquiry into the reported foreign intelligence effort remains open. Huang has also been asked to meet House investigators on December 1 and testify before a Senate panel on December 3.

#4854. Strategy buys 457 Bitcoin, spends $212M on preferred share buybacks

Strategy raised its preferred stock buyback limit to $4 billion and authorized the transfer of up to $1 billion from its dollar reserve.

Michael Saylor’s Strategy bought 457 Bitcoin for $29.6 million last week and spent $211.8 million buying back its STRC preferred shares, more than seven times what it spent on Bitcoin.

The company paid an average of $64,898 per Bitcoin (BTC) in the week ended Sunday, bringing its holdings to 851,305 BTC, according to Monday’s filing with the US Securities and Exchange Commission. Strategy paid $64.23 billion for those holdings, or an average of $75,446 per Bitcoin.

Strategy raised $176.7 million in net proceeds from sales of its common stock. It used $29.6 million for Bitcoin purchases and $147 million to buy back STRC shares. It funded another $64.8 million in buybacks with cash.

Strategy’s board also raised the limit on preferred stock buybacks from $3 billion to $4 billion, leaving $1.12 billion available for further purchases. The board authorized management to move up to $1 billion out of the reserve it keeps for dividends and interest payments. Strategy can use that cash for other purposes, including buying Bitcoin.

Bitcoin fell to $55,081 on Saturday, its lowest price since September 2024, before rebounding above $61,000 on Monday. Crypto investor Kyle Chassé estimated Saturday that Strategy’s Bitcoin holdings were worth roughly $47 billion, about $17 billion less than the company paid for them.

#4853. Trump-linked American Bitcoin secures $150M credit line from FalconX

The miner can borrow against its Bitcoin to fund operations and expansion after selling about 465 BTC to cover expenses in the third quarter.

American Bitcoin, the miner co-founded by Eric Trump, said in a securities filing Monday that its subsidiary secured a $150 million revolving credit line from FalconX using some of its Bitcoin as collateral.

The subsidiary, American Bitcoin Operating, signed the agreement Wednesday and had yet to borrow against the credit line as of Monday’s filing.

The company said it could use the funds to expand and cover operating costs without selling Bitcoin. In the third quarter, it sold about 465 Bitcoin (BTC) for $36.3 million to cover operating expenses.

The two-year credit line carries a 5.95% annual fee on borrowed amounts and a separate commitment fee on the unused balance. The borrower must initially pledge $1.43 worth of Bitcoin for every dollar borrowed. FalconX can demand more collateral if its value falls below $1.30 per dollar owed.

CEO Mike Ho said the credit line would let the miner “deploy capital and secure the infrastructure that supports our business, while we stay focused on our long-term Bitcoin accumulation strategy.” Eric Trump told investors on the company’s Nov. 4 earnings call that its reserve had surpassed 8,500 BTC.

American Bitcoin (ABTC) shares traded at $3.84 in premarket trading at 11:10 am UTC Monday, up 9.7% from Friday’s $3.50 close, according to Yahoo Finance. American Bitcoin shares were down about 94% from a year earlier, adjusted for July’s 1-for-15 reverse stock split. The company said it carried out the split to maintain compliance with Nasdaq’s minimum bid price requirement.
